## topic group2 6-22 | Dec 2025–Feb 2026: Science and technology in news - space missions, defence tests, health and biotech developments
### part 0: Semiconductors, Processors and Supercomputing
[0] h: DHRUV64
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] What | **India's first fully indigenous 1.0 GHz, 64-bit dual-core microprocessor**, by **C-DAC** under the **Microprocessor Development Programme (MDP)**
  [1.1] Architecture | Open-source **RISC-V** (no licence costs); the **third chip fabricated under the Digital India RISC-V (DIR-V) Programme**
  [1.2] Uses | 5G infrastructure, automotive, consumer electronics, industrial automation, IoT
  [1.3] Next | **Dhanush and Dhanush+** (DHANUSH64, DHANUSH64+ SoC)
  [1.4] India's share | Uses about **20% of the world's microprocessors**; has **20%** of the world's chip design engineers
[2] h: Indigenous processors – who made what
[3] table: Processor | Year | Developer | Use / note
  [3.0] SHAKTI | 2018 | IIT Madras | Strategic, space, defence; first open-source RISC-V family
  [3.1] AJIT | 2018 | IIT Bombay with SAMEER | Industrial, robotics
  [3.2] VIKRAM (3201) | 2025 | ISRO-SCL | Space navigation, guidance
  [3.3] THEJAS32 | – | C-DAC | Made at Silterra, Malaysia
  [3.4] THEJAS64 | 2025 | C-DAC | Industrial automation; made at SCL Mohali
  [3.5] VEGA | 2023 | C-DAC | C-DAC's first indigenous RISC-V processor series
  [3.6] DHRUV64 | 2025-26 | C-DAC | 1.0 GHz, 64-bit dual-core
[4] b: **SHAKTI-based aerospace chip "IRIS"** (Indigenous RISC-V Controller for Space Applications), **February 2025** – jointly developed and booted by **IIT Madras and ISRO** (specs by ISRO's IISU, fabricated at **SCL Chandigarh**); built on the open-source **RISC-V** ISA (SHAKTI is part of MeitY's **DIR-V** push); third SHAKTI chip after RIMO (**2018**) and MOUSHIK (**2020**).
[5] x: **SHAKTI** = IIT Madras; **VIKRAM** = ISRO-SCL; **VEGA** and **DHRUV64** = C-DAC.
[6] h: Semiconductor plants (ISM) – where
[7] table: Plant | Location
  [7.0] **CG Power** (CG Semi, with Renesas) – OSAT | **Sanand, Gujarat** (inaugurated **4 July 2026**)
  [7.1] **Tata Semiconductor Assembly and Test** – OSAT | **Jagiroad, Assam**
  [7.2] **HCL–Foxconn JV, India Chip Pvt. Ltd.** | **Jewar, Uttar Pradesh** (near Noida International Airport; foundation stone **21 Feb 2026**)
  [7.3] **SiCSem Pvt. Ltd.** – silicon-carbide fab | **Bhubaneswar, Odisha**
  [7.4] Tata Semiconductor Manufacturing – fab | Dholera, Gujarat
  [7.5] Micron – ATMP | Sanand, Gujarat
[8] h: Programmes and chips in news
[9] table: Programme | Launched | Key facts
  [9.0] India Semiconductor Mission (ISM) | December 2021 | **10** projects in six States, **₹1.60 lakh crore** (early 2026)
  [9.1] DIR-V | April 2022 | Indigenous RISC-V processors (MeitY)
  [9.2] Chips to Startup (C2S) | 2022, MeitY | Semiconductor design talent; **113** institutions; **₹250 crore** (5 years); **85,000** professionals
  [9.3] INUP-i2i | MeitY | **8,000+** trained; **348** short-term, **220** mid-term projects
[10] table: Fact | Key detail
  [10.0] **2-nanometre chip** (by **Qualcomm**) | Unveiled by **Ashwini Vaishnaw** in Bengaluru – **20-30 billion** transistors; ~**315** institutions in semiconductor programmes
  [10.1] **Maia 200** | **Microsoft's** 2nd-generation AI chip, **3 nm** TSMC; **first deployed in Iowa**
  [10.2] **PARAM Rudra** | **3.1 petaflop** indigenous supercomputer at **IIT Madras**, inaugurated **8 January 2026**; C-DAC Rudra servers; National Supercomputing Mission
  [10.3] C-DAC and units | C-DAC est. **1988**, Pune; petaflop = **10^15** operations/sec
  [10.4] **Military Quantum Mission Policy Framework** | Released **22 January 2026** by **CDS Gen Anil Chauhan**; four pillars – quantum **communication, computing, sensing & metrology, materials & devices**; CDS post created **2019**
### part 1: Missiles, Weapons, Defence Deals and Drones (UAVs)
[0] h: Missiles and weapons
[1] table: System | Key detail
  [1.0] **LRAShM** (Long Range Anti-Ship Missile) | DRDO; hypersonic glide vehicle; range over 1,500 km; about Mach 10; target in 7–8 minutes; land and naval platforms; conventional or nuclear payload; shown at Republic Day Parade 2026
  [1.1] **Pralay** | DRDO's quasi-ballistic surface-to-surface missile; 150–500 km; warhead 500–1,000 kg; two consecutive tests off Odisha, January 2026
  [1.2] **Agni-3** | Road-mobile, nuclear-capable IRBM; about 3,000 km; tested from Chandipur (Feb 2026)
  [1.3] SFDR (Solid Fuel Ducted Ramjet) | Tested off Odisha; for long-range air-to-air missiles
  [1.4] VSHORAD | Shoulder-fired; shot down drones at Chandipur
  [1.5] MPATGM | Third-generation man-portable anti-tank missile; top-attack; tested 11 January 2026 at KK Ranges, Ahilyanagar; led by DRDL Hyderabad
  [1.6] Suryastra | ₹292.69 crore contract to Nibe Ltd (Pune) with Elbit Systems (Israel); India's first indigenous universal multi-calibre rocket launcher; 150 and 300 km; CEP under 5 m
  [1.7] Ramjet 155 mm shells | Army to be first in the world to deploy; range up 30–50%; usable with M-777; specific impulse > 4000 Ns/kg; IIT Madras + Army Technology Board
  [1.8] Pechora SAM upgrade | Indigenous upgrade by Alpha Design Technologies Ltd (ADTL), Bengaluru; V-600 missile; 4R90 Yatagan radar (five antennas); range 30–35.4 km; altitude 20 m to 20–25 km; radar ~100 km; engages two targets; kill probability ~92%
  [1.9] Pinaka modernisation | Tata Advanced Systems – battery command posts upgrade (order 6 January 2026) with 510 Advanced Base Workshop; 80%+ indigenous; guided rockets 120 km and 300 km
  [1.10] SWaDeS | DRDO Defence Laboratory Jodhpur – portable Sea Water Desalination System
  [1.11] SKOCH Gold | DRDO's D4 Counter-Drone System (LRDE, DLRL, CHESS, IRDE) and Dharashakti EW system (DLRL, IRDE, DEAL)
  [1.12] **Khorramshahr-4** | **Iranian ballistic missile**; footage released amid US–Iran tensions
  [1.13] **Frey Effect** | Exploited by **high-power microwave (HPM)** pulse systems to induce **phantom sounds** and **severe vertigo**
[2] b: Labs: LRDE Bengaluru; DLRL and CHESS Hyderabad; IRDE and DEAL Dehradun. DRDO est. 1958.
[3] h: Deals and budget
[4] table: Deal | Key detail
  [4.0] **114 Rafale** | **DAC approved about ₹3.60 lakh crore**; maker **Dassault Aviation**; some to be built in India; IAF already has **36**; sequence Defence Procurement Board → DAC (Rajnath Singh) → CCS
  [4.1] **8 Dornier 228** | ₹2,312 crore with **HAL** for the **Coast Guard** (Buy-Indian)
  [4.2] Counter-drone systems | **Indrajala Drone Defence** with **Sigma Advanced Systems (Hyderabad)**
  [4.3] **₹4,666 crore contracts** | **CQB carbines** (5.56x45 mm, ARDE Pune, made by **Bharat Forge**) + heavyweight torpedoes for the Navy
  [4.4] **V-BAT drones** | **Shield AI (US)** with Hivemind AI; Group-3 VTOL; Indian partner **JSW Defence** – USD 90 million plant in **Hyderabad**
  [4.5] **Adani–Embraer** MoU | Final assembly line of Embraer commercial jets in India (up to 150 seats); Minister K. Rammohan Naidu
  [4.6] **Army–IISc MoU** | 23 January 2026 – indigenous AI defence systems
  [4.7] **Navy SATCOM** | **BSNL + Viasat**; adds **Ka-band to L-band**; BSNL Chairman Robert J. Ravi
  [4.8] **DIME** (Depot Integration Management Edition) | Logistics platform launched 8 January 2026 with **BISAG-N** (est. 2001, Gandhinagar) – **first end-to-end logistics integration**
  [4.9] **Haldia naval detachment** | Process begun on the Hooghly River, West Bengal
[5] h: Drones and UAVs
[6] b: **Heron Mk II:** **MALE UAV** by **Israel Aerospace Industries (IAI)**; emergency procurement after **Operation Sindoor** (up to ₹300 crore per system); new Army and Air Force orders; the **Indian Navy is buying it for the first time** (replacing older **Searcher** UAVs); India and IAI are studying **co-production**.
[7] table: Heron Mk II | Key detail
  [7.0] Endurance | 24–45 hours
  [7.1] Altitude | Up to 35,000 ft
  [7.2] Speed | 150 knots
  [7.3] Payload | Up to 500 kg
  [7.4] Sensors | EO/IR, SAR, ELINT/COMINT/SIGINT, encrypted SATCOM; automated take-off/landing
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] **MAPS UAV** | **₹168 crore** order to NewSpace Research and Technologies (Bengaluru) – **Army's first fully solar-powered UAV**
  [8.1] UAV journey | Began in **1998** with the Israeli **Searcher Mark-I**; market ₹29 billion (2020) → projected ₹1.5 trillion by 2026; goal – global drone hub by 2030
  [8.2] **Drone Rules 2021** (amended 2022, 2023) | Forms 25 → 5; **approvals 72 → 4**; civilian drones up to **500 kg**; ~90% airspace Green Zone (up to 400 ft); **Remote Pilot Certificate by DGCA**; 2022 amendment – no DGCA pilot licence for non-commercial nano and micro drones
  [8.3] **Digital Sky** | Single-window airspace portal (**Green, Yellow, Red zones**); services moving to **eGCA**
  [8.4] Incentives | PLI for drones ₹120 crore; **GST on drones cut to 5%** (from 18%/28%) in September 2025
  [8.5] **Namo Drone Didi** (Nov 2023) | Drones to women SHGs – 1,094 drones distributed (500+ under the scheme); a Sitapur (UP) SHG member featured in the 110th Mann Ki Baat
  [8.6] **SVAMITVA** | **April 2020**; **Ministry of Panchayati Raj + Survey of India**; **rural abadi areas**; target **3.44 lakh villages**; 3.28 lakh surveyed (~95%); 2.76 crore property cards in 31 States/UTs
  [8.7] **NECTAR** (North East Centre for Technology Application and Reach) | Long-endurance drones for disasters
  [8.8] Railways | **West Central Railway** pioneered drone inspections; drones used in Operation Sindoor
  [8.9] **Drone Shakti Mission** | Under the Anusandhan National Research Foundation
[9] table: Drone data (Feb 2026) | Number
  [9.0] Drones registered (UIN) | 38,575
  [9.1] Remote Pilot Certificates | 39,890
  [9.2] DGCA-approved RPTOs | 244
[10] x: Fixed-wing UAVs need a runway or catapult; only multirotors/VTOL can hover or land vertically. Large UAVs (Heron, Predator, Reaper) run on engines, not batteries.
### part 2: India-AI Impact Summit 2026 and AI Platforms
[0] h: The Summit
[1] table: Particular | Key detail
  [1.0] Dates / venue | **16–21 February 2026** (scheduled 16–20 Feb, extended to 21 Feb with longer expo hours), **Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi**
  [1.1] Significance | First global AI summit hosted in the Global South
  [1.2] Organiser | MeitY with the IndiaAI Mission
  [1.3] Theme | "Sarvajan Hitaya, Sarvajan Sukhaya"
  [1.4] **Three Sutras** | People, **Planet**, Progress
  [1.5] Expo | India AI Impact Expo inaugurated by PM Modi on 16 February 2026; 300+ exhibitors from 30 countries, 10+ pavilions, 70,000 sq m
  [1.6] Participation | 20+ Heads of State, about 60 Ministers (from 45+ countries), 500+ AI leaders
  [1.7] Outcome | New Delhi Declaration on AI Impact – India + 88 countries/organisations at adoption (later 89–92; 92 by mid-2026); USD 200 bn+ commitments; 100+ countries took part; inspired by "Vasudhaiva Kutumbakam"
[2] table: Item | Key detail
  [2.0] **Seven Chakras** (summit thematic working groups) | Human Capital; Inclusion for Social Empowerment; Safe and Trusted AI; Resilience, Innovation & Efficiency; Science; Democratizing AI Resources; AI for Economic Growth & Social Good
  [2.1] Declaration's own seven Chakras (pillars) | Human Capital Development; **Access for Social Empowerment**; **Secure & Trusted AI**; Resilient & Efficient AI Systems; **AI for Science**; Democratizing AI Resources; Economic Growth & Social Good
  [2.2] **Charter for the Democratic Diffusion of AI** | A **voluntary, non-binding** mechanism announced with the Declaration (with Global AI Impact Commons, Trusted AI Commons, International Network of AI for Science Institutions)
  [2.3] **MANAV Vision** | Announced by PM Modi – Modi Artificial Intelligence – Nation Aligned Vision: human-centric AI governance, **"AI for All"**, **data sovereignty**
  [2.4] Lead-up | Regional AI conferences in 8 States (Oct 2025–Jan 2026); 700+ global proposals; AI Compendium released
[3] x: Three **Sutras** = People, Planet, Progress; the seven **Chakras** are the working groups; the **Charter** is non-binding.
[4] table: Global Impact Challenge | Key detail
  [4.0] **AI for ALL** | With Startup India, Bhashini; up to ₹2.5 crore
  [4.1] **AI by HER** | Women – NITI Aayog WEP
  [4.2] **YUVAi** | Ages 13–21, with MyBharat and NIELIT; up to ₹85 lakh
  [4.3] Research symposium | Knowledge partner **IIIT Hyderabad** (~250 submissions)
[5] h: AI platforms launched
[6] table: Platform | Key detail
  [6.0] SAHI | Strategy for Artificial Intelligence in Healthcare for India – launched by J.P. Nadda, 17 February 2026, at the Summit
  [6.1] BODH | Benchmarking Open Data Platform for Health AI – IIT Kanpur + National Health Authority (NHA est. 2019, CEO Sunil Kumar Barnwal); linked to ABDM (15 August 2020), National Health Policy 2017
  [6.2] VoicERA | Open-source Voice AI stack by MeitY, built by the Digital India Bhashini division (Bhashini 2022; Digital India Corporation 2017)
  [6.3] BharatGen | Sovereign generative AI – now 15 languages, to cover all 22 scheduled languages; IIT Bombay ecosystem; DST-supported; BharatGen PARAM-2 17B (multilingual); Gnani.ai's Vachana is another Indian model
  [6.4] Sabhasaar | AI minutes of Gram Sabha meetings – launched 14 August 2025; 1.11 lakh+ GPs; Ministry of Panchayati Raj
  [6.5] NeophyteID | AI app (Kerala) to identify invasive plants – Malabar Botanical Garden and Institute for Plant Sciences, Kozhikode
  [6.6] SAMRIDH | MeitY start-up accelerator (2021) – 300 start-ups in 3 years
  [6.7] SUJVIKA | AI biotech trade-intelligence portal – launched on DBT's 40th foundation day; with ABLE (2003, Bengaluru); DBT est. 1986
  [6.8] Telangana AI Innovation Hub (TAIH) | Launched by CM A. Revanth Reddy at WEF Davos, 20 January 2026 – "world's first global AI experimentation platform"; pillars Talent Foundry, Innovation Engine, Capital Flywheel, Impact Labs
  [6.9] **Moltbook** | Platform **exclusively for AI agents** (Agentic AI); humans limited to a **read-only** observer role
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] **AI companions** | Synthetic personas acting as friends, therapists, partners – the **"loneliness economy"**; concerns: emotional dependency, behavioural nudging, no regulation (treated as entertainment)
  [7.1] **BSNL VoWiFi** | Rolled out across **all circles on 1 January 2026**; **free**; **IMS-based** (not circuit-switched); voice/SMS over Wi-Fi using VoIP with the existing number; BSNL est. 2000, HQ New Delhi
  [7.2] **IRDPP** | **DSIR** relaxed recognition norms for **deep-tech startups** under the Industrial Research and Development Promotion Programme
### part 3: Energy Materials – Batteries and Rare-earth Elements
[0] h: Sodium-ion vs lithium-ion
[1] table: Feature | Lithium-ion | Sodium-ion
  [1.0] Current collector | **Copper** (anode side) | **Aluminium** on both electrodes
  [1.1] Anode / cathode | Anode = **graphite**; cathodes **LiCoO2, LiMn2O4, LiFePO4** | Sodium from **soda ash**; layered oxides outperform polyanionic and Prussian blue analogues
  [1.2] Transport | Shipped at **30%** charge or less | Stored and shipped at **zero volts**; not classed as "Dangerous Goods"
  [1.3] Safety | – | Lower **thermal-runaway** risk
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] Li-ion price | ~**$1,100/kWh** (early 2010s) → ~**$108/kWh** (2025)
  [2.1] **PLI for Advanced Chemistry Cells (2021)** | ~**40 GWh** allocated; only ~**1 GWh** commissioned
  [2.2] Sodium-ion global capacity | ~**70 GWh** (2025) → about **400 GWh by 2030**
  [2.3] Sodium-ion cost | Expected to undercut Li-ion costs by **2035**
[3] x: **Graphite** is the **anode** of a Li-ion cell.
[4] h: Rare-earth elements (REEs)
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Number | **17 elements = 15 lanthanides (lanthanum to lutetium) + scandium and yttrium** (Group 3)
  [5.1] Abundance | **Not geologically rare** (**cerium** is as abundant as copper); rarity is economic and technological (hard separation)
  [5.2] Critical minerals, outside the REEs | **Lithium, cobalt, gallium, germanium**
  [5.3] Magnetism and phosphor emission | From **localised 4f electrons**
  [5.4] Ores | **Bastnäsite, monazite, ion-adsorption clays** (may contain thorium / uranium)
[6] table: Use | Elements
  [6.0] Permanent magnets (**NdFeB**) | **Neodymium** – EV motors, wind turbines
  [6.1] Phosphors (LEDs, flat-screen TVs, monitors) | **Europium, terbium, yttrium** – phosphorescent properties
  [6.2] Lasers, fibre optics | **Neodymium, erbium**
  [6.3] Refining catalysts | **Lanthanum, cerium**
[7] table: Country (USGS) | Reserves (million tonnes REO)
  [7.0] **China** | **44**
  [7.1] Brazil | **21**
  [7.2] **India** | **6.9**
  [7.3] World | Over **90**
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] **China** | Highest share in mining; about **91%** of separation / refining; **94%** of sintered permanent magnets
  [8.1] Uses | AI, robotics, defence and space
  [8.2] **Japan** | Plans to extract REE-rich mud from **6 km** underwater near **Minamitori Island** (early **2026**)
### part 4: Space – ISRO Missions, Launches and Space Law
[0] h: PSLV-C62 failure
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Launch | **12 January 2026**, 10:18 a.m., Sriharikota – **EOS-N1** mission by **NSIL**
  [1.1] Failure | **Third-stage anomaly** – **all 15 satellites lost** (7 Indian, 8 from France, Nepal, Brazil, UK)
  [1.2] Payload | **DRDO's "Anvesh"** surveillance satellite (detects targets under foliage/bunkers) and **Dhruva Space** satellites
  [1.3] Context | After **PSLV-C61/EOS-09 failed in May 2025** (also the 3rd stage) – **two PSLV failures in a row**
[2] h: Other ISRO and Indian space news
[3] b: **SSLV improved third stage (SS3):** static ground test at **SDSC Sriharikota** (Dec 2025) for **108 seconds**; all parameters matched – **qualified for flight**.
[4] table: SSLV / SS3 fact | Key detail
  [4.0] SSLV | Three-stage, all-solid; up to about 500 kg to LEO; launch-on-demand
  [4.1] SS3 | Gives up to 4 km/s velocity
  [4.2] Carbon-epoxy motor case | Payload gain about 90 kg; made at VSSC Composites Entity; cast and tested at SDSC
  [4.3] Solid Motor production facilities | July 2025, Sriharikota; SMPST tested the solid motor for the first orbital launch vehicle by an Indian start-up
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] **IN-SPACe Satellite Bus as a Service (SBaaS)** | **Astrome Technologies, Agnista Industries, Dhruva Space** – ₹5 crore each; IN-SPACe est. 2020, HQ **Bopal** (Ahmedabad), chair Pawan Kumar Goenka
  [5.1] **DEX instrument** | **PRL Ahmedabad**; detected **interplanetary dust**; flew on the **PSLV Orbital Experimental Module (POEM)**
  [5.2] **DefSAT 2026** | 4th edition, 24–26 February 2026, Manekshaw Centre, New Delhi; ~500 delegates, ~20 countries; SIA-India (President Subba Rao Pavuluri)
[6] h: World space and space security
[7] table: Item | Key detail
  [7.0] **China's TPG-1000C** | High-power **microwave weapon** – Northwest Institute of Nuclear Technology (team of Wang Gang); about **20 GW for about 1 minute**; can disrupt LEO satellites
  [7.1] **Space-debris impact prediction using seismometer networks** | Sonic booms – **Johns Hopkins University**; tested on Shenzhou-15 debris (2 April 2024) with 125 seismometers in California
  [7.2] **US Lunar Fission Surface Power Project** | Small fission reactor on the Moon by the **early 2030s** for NASA's **Artemis Base Camp** – the **first permanent nuclear power source beyond Earth orbit**
  [7.3] **CHEOPS (ESA)** | Identified a **four-planet system** around **LHS 1903** with an anomalous **rocky outermost planet**
  [7.4] **TeraWave** | Satellite constellation planned by **Blue Origin**
  [7.5] **New Norcia 3 (NNO3)** | Deep-space communication antenna inaugurated by the **European Space Agency (ESA)** – a **'permanent space phone line'**
[8] table: Technology | Key point
  [8.0] Compact fission reactor | Shipping-container size; 10–100 kW; NASA KRUSTY demo up to 10 kW
  [8.1] Nuclear Thermal Propulsion (NTP) | Reactor heats propellant (liquid hydrogen); high thrust; US DRACO test by 2026
  [8.2] Nuclear Electric Propulsion (NEP) | Reactor electricity powers ion/plasma thrusters; low continuous thrust
  [8.3] **RTG** (Radioisotope Thermoelectric Generator) | Turns the decay heat of plutonium-238 into electricity; a few hundred watts; used on deep-space probes
[9] table: Space law | Key point
  [9.0] Outer Space Treaty 1967, Article IV | Bans nuclear weapons/WMDs in orbit and on celestial bodies; peaceful nuclear power is allowed
  [9.1] UN Principles on Nuclear Power Sources in Outer Space, 1992 | UNGA Resolution 47/68; non-binding; NTP/NEP propulsion not covered
  [9.2] 1972 Liability Convention | Absolute liability for damage on Earth or to aircraft in flight; fault-based in outer space
  [9.3] COPUOS | UN Committee on the Peaceful Uses of Outer Space
[10] b: **NPT Nuclear Weapon States:** **USA, Russia, UK, France, China**; India, Pakistan and Israel are not recognised. NSG membership does not mean automatic NPT membership.

### part 6: Ships, Exercises and Army Initiatives
[0] h: Ships and vessels
[1] table: Platform | Key detail
  [1.0] **INS Anjadeep** | Commissioned at **Chennai Port** on **27 February 2026** – Anti-Submarine Warfare ship by **GRSE, Kolkata**; sonar **Abhay**; **77 m**; up to **25 knots**
  [1.1] **'Krishna'** (Yard **18003**) | India's first indigenous **Cadet Training Ship**; launched at **L&T Kattupalli**; **122 m**, ~**4,700 t**, **20 knots**; named after the Krishna river
  [1.2] **Samudra Pratap** | Coast Guard's first indigenous **pollution control vessel**; inducted **5 January 2026** at Goa Shipyard; **60%+** indigenous
  [1.3] **INSV Kaundinya** | **"Stitched ship"** (no nails, coir ropes) – reached **Muscat** from **Porbandar**, ~**1,400 km** in **17 days** under sail; design from **5th-century Ajanta paintings**; built by **Babu Shankaran** with Hodi Innovations
[2] h: Exercises
[3] table: Exercise | Partners / forces | Venue and dates

### part 8: Health – HIV, Vaccines, Neurotechnology and Biosecurity
[0] h: Lenacapavir (Yeztugo)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Drug | **First twice-yearly injectable PrEP for HIV**, by **Gilead Sciences** (est. 1987, Foster City); brand **"Yeztugo"**
  [1.1] Approval | **US FDA, 18 June 2025** – **world's first capsid-based HIV inhibitor**
  [1.2] Trials | **100%** effective in PURPOSE 1 (zero infections in women); **99.9%** in PURPOSE 2
  [1.3] Use | A PrEP drug, not a vaccine; only for **HIV-negative** persons; does not replace **ART**
  [1.4] **Zimbabwe rollout** | Began **19 February 2026** at **Epworth** (~20 km south of Harare); 46,000 doses, 24 sites; funded by the US government and Global Fund; among **9 countries** chosen by WHO for early access
  [1.5] **Zimbabwe PrEP order** | Oral PrEP (**2016**) → dapivirine vaginal ring (**2021**) → injectable cabotegravir (**2024**) → lenacapavir (**2026**)
  [1.6] Science | Resistant HIV replicates at only 20–30% of normal – the **capsid** is a high-constraint target (capsid shape paper 1999, Science)
  [1.7] **AZT (zidovudine), 1987** | First HIV drug; targeted **reverse transcriptase**
  [1.8] Global | ~41 million living with HIV; ~27 million (67%) in Sub-Saharan Africa; 44 million+ deaths since the 1980s
  [1.9] **Tattooing** | Can transmit **Hepatitis B and HIV**
[2] h: Vaccines and diseases
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **Indigenous tetanus and Td vaccines** | Launched by **J.P. Nadda** at **CRI Kasauli** (est. **1905**); under the **UIP (1985)**
  [3.1] **KFD vaccine** | Phase-I human trials, ICMR-supported
  [3.2] **Kyasanur Forest Disease** | Tick *Haemaphysalis spinigera*, monkeys; no person-to-person spread; fatality 3–10%
  [3.3] **ZyCoV-D** | **World's first DNA-based COVID-19 vaccine**, supported by the **National Biopharma Mission**
  [3.4] **Zycubo** (copper histidinate injection) | Approved by the **US FDA** – **world's first treatment for Menkes disease** in children
  [3.5] **Menkes disease** | Rare **X-linked genetic disorder** affecting **copper absorption**
[4] h: Neurotechnology
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] **UNESCO (2025)** | **World's first comprehensive ethical framework for neurotechnology**; bans use of neural data for manipulative/political/commercial targeting and coercive collection for employment/insurance
  [5.1] BCI types | **Non-invasive – EEG headsets**; **invasive – implanted electrodes**
  [5.2] India | **Stroke is the largest contributor to neurological disability**; IIT Kanpur – BCI-based robotic hand for stroke patients; National Brain Research Centre (Manesar); IISc Brain Research Centre
  [5.3] **Dognosis** | Start-up decoding dog brain signals to detect cancer

[8] h: Biosecurity
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] **Biosecurity** / **biosafety** | Prevents **intentional misuse** / prevents **accidental** release
  [9.1] **Biological Weapons Convention (BWC)** | **1975** – first treaty banning development, production and stockpiling of bioweapons; India is a member of the **BWC and the Australia Group**
  [9.2] **Ricin** | Toxin from **castor**
  [9.3] Global Health Security Index | India **66th**
  [9.4] **Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety** | Under the CBD; **legally binding**; on **Living Modified Organisms**; **adopted in Montreal on 29 January 2000**
  [9.5] **Environment (Protection) Act, 1986** | Parent law for hazardous microorganisms and GMOs; the Biosafety Rules, 1989 were made under it
  [9.6] **BHISHM Cube to Sri Lanka** (2 December 2025) | Under **Project Aarogya Maitri** – a working medical centre (OPD, laboratory, X-ray, minor operation theatre) at **Indivitiya, Ja-Ela**, in **cyclone-hit Sri Lanka**
[10] table: India's framework | Role
  [10.0] Biosafety Rules, 1989 | Genetically engineered organisms
  [10.1] Recombinant DNA Research Guidelines, 2017 | Biocontainment
  [10.2] WMD Act, 2005 | Criminalises biological weapons
  [10.3] DBT, NCDC, Plant Quarantine Organisation | Lab governance, surveillance, plant health
[11] table: Country/region | Biosecurity measure
  [11.0] USA | National Biodefense Strategy (2022–28); 2024 Synthetic DNA Screening Guidance
  [11.1] EU | Health Security Framework (2022)
  [11.2] China | Biosecurity Law (2021)
  [11.3] Australia | Biosecurity Act (2015)
  [11.4] UK | Biological Security Strategy (2023)

[8] h: PERT and other genome tools
[9] table: PERT | Key detail
  [9.0] Full form | **Prime-Editing-mediated Readthrough of Premature Termination codons**
  [9.1] Method | **Edits a tRNA gene into a suppressor tRNA**
  [9.2] Published | In **Nature**, by **Broad Institute, Harvard, University of Minnesota**
  [9.3] Target | **Nonsense mutations** (premature stop codon, e.g. **TAG**) – about **25%** of disease-causing genetic changes
  [9.4] tRNA genes | Human cells have **418 tRNA genes**; **4 promising** (leucine, arginine, tyrosine, serine)
  [9.5] Efficiency | **60–80%**, against **10–20%** for HDR
  [9.6] Mouse models | Batten, Tay-Sachs, Niemann-Pick C1
[10] table: Tool | Key detail
  [10.0] **AlphaGenome** | **Google DeepMind**; predicts DNA mutations and gene regulation
  [10.1] **DisoBind** | **NCBS (TIFR)** deep-learning tool for **intrinsically disordered proteins**; NCBS est. **1992**, TIFR **1945**
  [10.2] **PATH-GENI** | Open-source software (Ministry of S&T) for **ligand unbinding pathways** and **drug residence time**
[11] b: **Biomaterials**: **drop-in** (chemically identical to conventional, e.g. **bio-PET**), **drop-out** (chemically different, e.g. **PLA**), novel (self-healing, bioactive); India's bioplastics market ~**USD 500 million** (**2024**).

[3] h: Other research
[4] table: Development | Key detail
  [4.0] **Mpemba effect** explained | **JNCASR Bengaluru** – world's first supercomputer simulation of hot water freezing faster; named after Tanzanian **Erasto Mpemba** (**1969**); noted earlier by Aristotle, Bacon, Descartes
  [4.1] **Deuteron** (ALICE, CERN, LHC) | ALICE explained how deuterons survive collisions; deuteron = nucleus of deuterium – **1** proton + **1** neutron; charge **+1**, spin **1**; uses – heavy water, fusion fuel, tritium production
  [4.2] **National Environmental Standards Laboratory** | At **CSIR-NPL** **80th** Foundation Day, by **Jitendra Singh** – calibration of pollution monitors; India second after the UK
  [4.3] **Water from air** | Nobel laureate **Omar Yaghi** – molecular sponges; **1,000+** litres/day; works below **20%** humidity; solar-powered
  [4.4] **Photo-rechargeable supercapacitor** | DST scientists – **nickel-cobalt oxide** nanowires on nickel foam
  [4.5] **Mangrove clam** (*Geloina erosa*) | **ICAR-CMFRI** achieved induced breeding – **"Kandal Kakka"** in north Kerala
  [4.6] **New biosurfactant** | **IASST Guwahati** (DST) – antimicrobial, for cosmetics / pharma
  [4.7] **INSA–CSIR-NIScPR MoU** | Science communication and STI policy; INSA President **Shekhar C. Mande**

[4] h: Easily confused facts
[5] table: Topic | Key detail
  [5.0] Indigenous processors | **SHAKTI = IIT Madras**; **AJIT = IIT Bombay + SAMEER**; **VIKRAM = ISRO-SCL**; **DHRUV64 and VEGA = C-DAC**
  [5.1] AI Summit | Sutras = **People, Planet, Progress**; theme "Sarvajan Hitaya, Sarvajan Sukhaya"
  [5.2] **Lenacapavir** | A preventive drug, **not a vaccine**; only for HIV-negative persons; ART still needed
  [5.3] **BoPS** | Under the **Ministry of Ports, Shipping and Waterways**; modelled on **BCAS**; CISF = Recognised Security Organisation
  [5.4] **Outer Space Treaty Art. IV** | Bans nuclear weapons / WMDs; peaceful nuclear power allowed; the **1992** UN Principles are **non-binding**
  [5.5] **Cartagena Protocol** | Adopted in **Montreal (29 Jan 2000)**; **BWC = 1975**; biosecurity = intentional, biosafety = accidental
  [5.6] REEs | **Lithium, cobalt** are critical minerals; REEs = **15** lanthanides + Sc + Y; Li-ion **anode = graphite**
  [5.7] **SVAMITVA** | Maps **rural abadi** areas; Ministry of Panchayati Raj + Survey of India
  [5.8] UAVs | **Fixed-wing UAVs cannot hover** or land vertically; Heron Mk II is by **IAI (Israel)**
  [5.9] Ports and MILAN | **Largest container port = JNPT**; **MILAN** began in **1995 at Port Blair**

### part 1: Navy – Warships, Fleet Reviews and Sailing Expeditions
[0] h: INS Udaygiri and INS Himgiri
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Commissioning | Together on **26 August 2025 at Visakhapatnam** – Nilgiri-class (**Project 17A**) stealth guided-missile frigates
  [1.1] First | **First time two major warships from two different shipyards were commissioned together**
  [1.2] **Udaygiri** | **100th ship designed by the Navy's Warship Design Bureau**
[2] table: Ship | Builder | Named after | Command | Motto
  [2.0] **INS Udaygiri** | **MDL, Mumbai** | Mountain range in **Andhra Pradesh** | Eastern Naval Command (Sunrise Fleet) | Sanyuktaha Paramojayaha
  [2.1] **INS Himgiri** | **GRSE, Kolkata** | Himalayas | Western Naval Command | Adrushyam Ajayam
[3] h: Project 17A
[4] table: Spec | Key detail
  [4.0] Length / displacement | **149 m** / ~**6,670** tonnes
  [4.1] Speed | **28** knots
  [4.2] Weapons | **8 BrahMos** VLS, LRSAM / Barak 8, torpedoes, rocket launchers, **127 mm** gun, **2 AK-630** CIWS
  [4.3] Sensors | **Humsa (NG)** sonar; **'Shakti'** EW suite
[5] b: Other **2025** commissionings: **INS Surat** (destroyer), **INS Vaghsheer** (submarine), **INS Arnala** (ASW craft), **INS Nistar** (diving support vessel).
[6] h: INS Tamal and INS Ikshak
[7] table: Ship | Key detail
  [7.0] **INS Tamal** | Commissioned **1 July 2025** at **Kaliningrad**, Russia; built at **Yantar Shipyard**; **last warship procured from a foreign source**; **eighth** Krivak / Talwar-class frigate from Russia in two decades
  [7.1] INS Tamal – service | **Western Fleet** ("Sword Arm"); **3,900 t**; crew **250+**; BrahMos; **26%** indigenous, **33+** Made-in-India systems; crew trained in St. Petersburg and Kaliningrad
  [7.2] **INS Ikshak** | Commissioned **6 November 2025**, **Kochi**, by CNS Admiral **Dinesh K. Tripathi**; **third Survey Vessel (Large)**; built by **GRSE, Kolkata** (>**80%** indigenous)
  [7.3] INS Ikshak – features | **First SVL with dedicated accommodation for women**; dual role – hydrographic survey + HADR / hospital ship; name means **"The Guide"**
[8] b: Two **Triput-class** frigates are being built at **Goa Shipyard Ltd** with Russian design support; fleet total **10** ships of similar capability across four classes.
[9] b: BrahMos was on **INS Kolkata** and Talwar-class ships before INS Tamal.
[10] b: **INS Anjadip** (commissioned **23 Dec 1972**) – the **last Petya-class** (Arnala-class) warship; decommissioned **13 December 2003 at Visakhapatnam** after **31** years. Its **last Commanding Officer, Cdr Siddhartha Panda**, handed the ensign box to the FOC-in-C Eastern Naval Command, **Vice Adm O.P. Bansal**.

[14] h: IASV Triveni
[15] table: Fact | Key detail
  [15.0] Expedition | **Samudra Pradakshina** – **first-ever tri-service all-women sailing circumnavigation** – started **11 September 2025**
  [15.1] Vessel | **Indian Army Sailing Vessel Triveni** – **50-foot yacht** built at the **Indian Naval Dockyard, Puducherry**
  [15.2] Crew | **10 women officers – 5 Army, 1 Navy, 4 Air Force**; leader **Lt Col Anuja Varudkar**; Sqn Ldr Shraddha Raju (Su-30 technical officer); Lt Cdr Priyanka Gusain
  [15.3] Route | About **21,600 nautical miles** over **8–9 months**; back to **Mumbai by May 2026**; crosses the **equator twice**
  [15.4] Three Great Capes | **Cape Leeuwin** (Australia), **Cape Horn** (S. America), **Cape of Good Hope** (S. Africa); hardest stretch – **Drake Passage**
  [15.5] Ports | Australia, New Zealand, Argentina, South Africa
### part 2: Air Defence, Fighter Aircraft and Defence Deals
[0] h: Integrated Air Defence Weapon System (IADWS)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Test | **Maiden flight tests on 23 August 2025** off the Odisha coast (**Chandipur Integrated Test Range**)
  [1.1] Components | **QRSAM** (Quick Reaction SAM) + **VSHORADS** (very short range, shoulder-fired) + **Directed Energy Weapon (high-power laser)**
  [1.2] Control | **Centralised Command and Control Centre by DRDL**; labs – DRDL, Research Centre Imarat, **CHESS**
  [1.3] Result | Destroyed **three targets** (two fixed-wing UAVs + one multi-copter drone) **simultaneously**
  [1.4] Origin | **Fully indigenous**
[2] table: Air defence layer | Systems
  [2.0] Long | Barak-8, S-400
  [2.1] Medium | Akash, MR-SAM
  [2.2] Short | QRSAM, Spyder
  [2.3] Very short | VSHORADS, MANPADS
[3] h: Fighter aircraft
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] **LCA Tejas Mk1A** | Built by **HAL**; IAF to receive the first in **October 2025**; first flight witnessed by **Rajnath Singh at Nashik, 17 October 2025**
  [4.1] Contract | **83-aircraft** contract; deliveries due **February 2024**, delayed by **GE Aerospace engine supply** (**1–2** per month)
  [4.2] Upgrades | **AESA radar, EW suite, better avionics**
  [4.3] IAF strength | **29 squadrons vs 42 sanctioned**; needs **2** squadrons a year
  [4.4] Rafale | **18** off-the-shelf expected; Dassault has **233** in production (**4**/month); MRFA proposal
  [4.5] **AMCA** | **First indigenous fifth-generation stealth fighter**, by the **Aeronautical Development Agency (ADA, DRDO)**; twin-engine; induction **by 2035**; flight-test centre at **Puttaparthi (AP)** (May **2026**)
  [4.6] **India-France (Safran) 120 kN engine** | Government-to-Government deal to co-develop the engine for **AMCA Mk-2** (Tranche **2**) over **~10 years**; **AMCA Mk-1 uses GE-414 (USA)**
  [4.7] Framework | **Horizon 2047** roadmap; **2026 – India-France Year of Innovation**
  [4.8] **Safran's Hyderabad facility** | MRO for the **Rafale M88 engine** – **first M88 hub outside France**
  [4.9] India-France ties | **36** Rafale; Scorpene submarines; ISRO-CNES
[5] table: Aircraft | Generation
  [5.0] Rafale (Dassault Aviation, France) | **4.5-generation**, twin-engine multirole
  [5.1] MiG-29 (Soviet) | **Fourth-generation**
  [5.2] Tejas Mk-1 | **Fourth-generation** (Mk1A, Mk2: **4.5**)
  [5.3] **AMCA** | **Fifth-generation** (indigenous)
  [5.4] F-22, F-35 (USA), Su-57 (Russia), J-20 (China) | **Fifth-generation** (stealth, sensor fusion)
[6] x: **AMCA** will be India's **first fifth-generation fighter**.
[7] h: Submarines and India-US defence
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] **Project 75(I)** | **MDL, Mumbai** began contract talks with **ThyssenKrupp Marine Systems (TKMS), Germany** for **six conventional submarines with Air-Independent Propulsion (AIP)**, with technology transfer
  [8.1] TKMS | Known for **Type 214 and Type 218**
  [8.2] Project 75 | MDL built the **Scorpene** class with France
  [8.3] India-Germany | Defence Cooperation Agreement: **2019**
  [8.4] Procurements from the US | **24 MH-60R** helicopters; **145 M777** howitzers; Sig Sauer **716** rifles; **GE F-414** engines for LCA Mk-2 (**80% ToT**); GE F-404 for Tejas Mk-1A
  [8.5] Visit | **USS Frank Cable** visited Chennai in August **2025**
  [8.6] **Theaterisation** | Debate on **integrated theatre commands** for the armed forces
[9] h: AP Drone City
[10] table: Fact | Key detail
  [10.0] **A.P. Drone Corporation** | Nodal agency – aim: make AP the **"Drone Capital of India"**; identified **100+ use cases**; EOI for drone-as-a-service (last date **21 April 2025**)
  [10.1] **Drone City – Orvakal, Kurnool district** | Over **300 acres**; master plan and land acquisition done; manufacturing, R&D, testing, training, incubation
### part 3: Semiconductors, Telecom and Digital Twins
[0] h: Vikram 32-bit processor (Semicon India 2025)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Presentation | **2 September 2025** – **Ashwini Vaishnaw** presented PM Modi the "Made in India" **Vikram 32-bit launch-vehicle-grade processor**
  [1.1] Developers | **Vikram Sarabhai Space Centre (ISRO) + Semiconductor Laboratory (SCL), Chandigarh**
  [1.2] **Vikram 3201** | **ISRO's first indigenous 32-bit space-grade microprocessor**, with **Kalpana 3201** (both from SCL Chandigarh)
  [1.3] Predecessor | **VIKRAM1601** (16-bit), in ISRO avionics since **2009**
  [1.4] Origin | Builds on ISRO's earlier work – outside the **Semicon India Programme**
  [1.5] Also shown | 31 prototype chips from IIT Jammu, IIT Roorkee, IIT Dhanbad, IIT Ropar, NIT Durgapur, NIT Calicut
[2] table: Fact | Key detail
  [2.0] **India Semiconductor Mission** | Launched **December 2021 under MeitY**; 5 fab units under construction; pilot line of one done; 2 more soon
  [2.1] Market | Global $1 trillion by 2030–35; India has **about 20% of the world's chip design engineers**
  [2.2] Indian-chip telecom system | Got **Telecommunication Engineering Centre (TEC)** certification (TEC is under the **DoT**, Ministry of Communications)
  [2.3] India's focus | **Mature nodes, 28 nm to 65 nm** (automotive, telecom, industrial electronics)
  [2.4] **Sangam: Digital Twin** | **Department of Telecommunications (DoT)**; EoI launched **15–16 February 2024**; 5G, IoT, AI and digital twins for **infrastructure planning**; two stages (exploration, then demonstrations)
  [2.5] **SAKSHAM-3000** | Indigenous high-capacity **switch-cum-router** (**25.6 Tbps**) by **C-DOT** (Centre for Development of Telematics)
[3] h: India Mobile Congress 2025 and the 4G/6G push
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] **IMC 2025** | **9th edition**, New Delhi; inaugurated by **PM Modi**; "Asia's largest" telecom event; organised by **DoT and COAI** (Cellular Operators Association of India); 400+ exhibitors; Startup World Cup 2025
  [4.1] Since 2014 | Electronics production 6-fold; mobile manufacturing 28-fold; data costs down 98%; 1 lakh Made-in-India 4G towers
[5] table: Indigenous 4G stack (BSNL) | Provider
  [5.0] Radio Access Network (RAN) | Tejas Networks
  [5.1] Core Network | C-DOT
  [5.2] Integration & data centres | TCS – 100,000+ sites; Cognitive Network Operations platform
[6] b: With it **India joined Denmark, Sweden, South Korea and China** as makers of their own 4G equipment; **5G-ready**; launched with the **100% 4G Saturation Mission** (Digital Bharat Nidhi, ~29,000–30,000 villages); export potential vs China's Digital Silk Road (Huawei, ZTE ~70% of Africa's 4G).
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] **6G vision** | ~**1 Tbps**, sub-millisecond latency, **terahertz** (100 GHz–1 THz); $1.2 trillion GDP contribution by 2035; 10% of global 6G patents
  [7.1] **6G Symposium** | Opened by **Jyotiraditya Scindia and Dr. Neeraj Mittal**
  [7.2] **Bharat 6G Alliance** | MoUs with **NASSCOM and the European Space Agency**; four whitepapers (spectrum roadmap, AI, architecture/security, RF sensing)
[8] table: Feature | 4G | 5G | 6G
  [8.0] Launch | 2009–2012 | 2019 onwards | 2030+
  [8.1] Peak speed | 100 Mbps mobile / 1 Gbps | 10–20 Gbps | 1 Tbps+
  [8.2] Latency | ~50 ms | ~1–10 ms | ~0.1 ms
  [8.3] Key tech | LTE, OFDMA, MIMO | mmWave, Massive MIMO, network slicing | Terahertz, AI-native
[9] b: **AWS outage (October 2025):** 1,000+ services hit (WhatsApp, Snapchat, Reddit, UK tax portal); origin **North Virginia** region – a **DNS resolution issue for DynamoDB** (serverless NoSQL database) endpoints; earlier, the 2024 Microsoft Azure and CrowdStrike outages.
### part 4: ISRO, Space Infrastructure and Space Law
[0] h: Chandrayaan-2's CHACE-2
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **CHACE-2** | Chandra's Atmospheric Composition Explorer-2 on the **Chandrayaan-2 orbiter**; by ISRO's **Space Physics Laboratory**
  [1.1] Finding | **First-ever direct observation of a Coronal Mass Ejection (CME) impact on the lunar exosphere** – dayside exosphere density rose by **more than one order of magnitude** (**sodium, potassium, argon** knocked off the regolith)
  [1.2] Chandrayaan-2 | Launched **22 July 2019** on **GSLV Mk-III-M1**; eight payloads; Vikram lander failed, orbiter operational
[2] h: National Space Day and the space-law gap
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **Second National Space Day** | **23 August 2025** – marks the **Chandrayaan-3** soft landing near the lunar south pole
  [3.1] Gap | India has ratified the UN space treaties but has **no comprehensive national space law** (no Space Activities Act); **IN-SPACe works without statutory authority**
  [3.2] Existing framework | Catalogue of Indian Standards for Space Industry (safety); **Indian Space Policy (2023)**; **IN-SPACe Norms, Guidelines and Procedures (NPG), 2023** – authorisation of commercial activities
  [3.3] **Outer Space Treaty (1967)** | Space is the "province of all mankind"; **Article VI – the state is responsible for private activities**; treaties are not self-executing
  [3.4] National space laws | **Japan, Luxembourg, US**
[4] h: Protecting India's satellites – "bodyguard satellites"
[5] table: Area | Key detail
  [5.0] Tracking | **IS4OM** – Indian Space Situational Awareness and Management Centre, **Bengaluru**; Project **NETRA**; Multi-Object Tracking Radar at Sriharikota; **10+** collision-avoidance manoeuvres in **2023**
  [5.1] Cyber | CERT-In guidelines (**2025**); IN-SPACe licensing; lesson from the **2022 Viasat cyberattack** (Ukraine)
  [5.2] Space weather | **Aditya-L1**; **CESSI (IISER Kolkata)**
  [5.3] Debris | Pledge of **"Debris-Free Space Missions by 2030"** at the **2024 IADC** meet (hosted by India); UN COPUOS guidelines (**2019**)
  [5.4] Fleet | **₹27,000 crore** programme for ~**52 surveillance satellites** from **2026**
### part 5: World Space and Astronomy
[0] h: Solar missions
[1] table: Mission | Key detail
  [1.0] **Solar Orbiter** (ESA-NASA, launched February **2020**) | First spacecraft to image the **Sun's poles**; first to trace **Solar Energetic Electrons (SEE)** to solar flares (bursts) and CMEs (gradual release); **300+** SEE bursts studied (**2020–22**)
  [1.1] **Parker Solar Probe** (NASA, **2018**) | Closest approach to the Sun
  [1.2] **Aditya-L1** (ISRO, **2023**) | India's first solar observatory, at Lagrange Point **L1**
[2] h: Stars and physics
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **UPM J1040-3551 AabBab** | Rare quadruple system: **two cold T-type brown dwarfs orbiting a pair of young red dwarfs** – first such arrangement seen (Monthly Notices of the Royal Astronomical Society, Sept **2025**)
  [3.1] **Brown dwarfs** | **"Failed stars"**: **cannot sustain hydrogen fusion**; up to **70 times Jupiter's mass**; companion chance below **5%**
  [3.2] **Red dwarfs** | **Most common** stars (**about 70% of Milky Way stars**); long-lived
  [3.3] **Blue supergiants** | Among the **hottest**, most luminous stars
[4] b: **Entangled atomic clocks** (Jacob Covey, Igor Pikovski, Johannes Borregaard; PRX Quantum, July **2025**): tabletop test of the quantum-gravity interface with **three entangled ytterbium clocks** at different elevations; entanglement in a **W state** (robust to particle loss, unlike GHZ); frequency shift ~**0.02 Hz** for **1 km**; coherence time ~**50 s**.
[5] table: Quantum gravity theory | Idea
  [5.0] String Theory | Extra dimensions and gravitons
  [5.1] Loop Quantum Gravity | Discrete spacetime at the Planck scale
### part 6: Military Exercises
[0] h: Military exercises
[1] table: Exercise | Partner | Key detail
  [1.0] **Konkan-25** | **UK** | Began **5 October 2025** off India's west coast (UK PM **Keir Starmer's** visit); **first-ever bilateral Carrier Strike Group exercise**
  [1.1] Konkan-25 – forces | UK | **INS Vikrant** (MiG-29K, Kamov-31, MH-60R, ALH, LCA-Navy) + **HMS Prince of Wales** (F-35B, Merlin / Wildcat); Sea and Harbour phases; named after the Konkan Coast; biennial since **2004**
  [1.2] **Yudh Abhyas 2025** (21st) | **US** | **Fort Wainwright, Alaska**, **1–14 September 2025**; India – battalion of the **Madras Regiment**; US – **1st** Battalion, **5th** Infantry Regiment 'Bobcats' (**11th** Airborne Division)
  [1.3] Yudh Abhyas – focus | US | Mountain warfare, heliborne operations, UAS / counter-UAS, UN peacekeeping; started **2004**, annual
  [1.4] **Bright Star 2025** | **Egypt** (under US CENTCOM) | **1–10 September 2025**; **INS Trikand** (Talwar-class stealth frigate) reached **Alexandria** on **1 Sep 2025**; India tri-service
  [1.5] Bright Star – participants | Egypt | India, Egypt, US, Saudi Arabia, Qatar, Greece, Cyprus, Italy; began **1980** as a US–Egypt bilateral
[2] b: Konkan-25 is linked with **India-UK Vision 2035**, the Regional Maritime Security Centre of Excellence (**RMSCE**) and **IPOI**.
### part 7: AI Policy, Education and Digital Society
[0] h: Rules and global governance
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **MeitY draft rules on AI-generated content** (22 October 2025) | Visual – label covering **at least 10% of the surface area**; audio – label for the **first 10% of duration**; platforms must take **user declarations and verify** them; **metadata for traceability**; feedback by 6 November 2025
  [1.1] Context | India ~1 billion internet users; petition by Abhishek Bachchan and Aishwarya Rai Bachchan against AI videos; similar – EU AI Act, China's synthetic media rules
  [1.2] **OpenAI** (Sam Altman, Feb 2025) | **India its second-largest market by users**
  [1.3] **UNGA** | Launched the **UN Independent International Scientific Panel on AI** (annual reports) and the **Global Dialogue on AI Governance** – sessions **July 2026, Geneva**, and **2027, New York**; linked to the **Global Digital Compact** (September 2024, Pact for the Future); UNSG Antonio Guterres – a **"pathbreaking milestone"**
  [1.4] **UNCPGA** (UN Council of Presidents of the General Assembly) | Report urging immediate international action on the risks and opportunities of **Artificial General Intelligence (AGI)**
  [1.5] **AI and news copyright** | LLMs trained on news without consent or compensation; **DPIIT** formed a committee on copyright and AI; **Australia and Canada** make tech giants pay publishers; **"fair use"** allows limited use without permission and **requires no payment**
[2] h: Research, education and work
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **SARAL** (Simplified and Automated Research Amplification and Learning) | By the **Anusandhan National Research Foundation (ANRF)**; AI (NLP) makes **layperson-friendly summaries of research papers** (videos, podcasts, posters, presentations); a communication tool, not a funding scheme
  [3.1] ANRF | India's newest science funding agency; about **70% of its budget from private sources**
  [3.2] **RDI Scheme** | **₹1 lakh crore** Research Development and Innovation Scheme, approved by Cabinet in **July 2025** (low-interest, long-tenure loans to private firms)
  [3.3] **AI in curriculum from Class 3** | Academic year **2026-27** – Ministry of Education; follows "Skilling for AI Readiness" (July 2025); **CBSE offers AI as a skill subject from Class 6**
  [3.4] Constraints | ~9% schools single-teacher; 35% have under 50 students and two teachers – **"unplugged curricula"** suggested
  [3.5] **Central Square Foundation** report | About **70% of teachers are tech-savvy** and use AI tools; **CBSE** warned against **ChatGPT in board exams**
  [3.6] **IndiaAI Mission pillars** | AI Centres of Excellence, IndiaAI Compute Capacity, IndiaAI FutureSkills, Application Development Initiative
  [3.7] **AI in healthcare** | AI forecast **child malnutrition in Kenya** up to **six months ahead**; IBM Watson, Exscientia (oncology drug discovery); Amazon Transcribe Medical; McKinsey – up to $100 billion/year savings in US healthcare; law – **DPDP Act 2023**
  [3.8] AI types | **Narrow = weak AI**; **General = strong AI**; Super AI
  [3.9] **Hidden human labour** | LLM training: self-supervised → **supervised (human labels)** → **reinforcement learning (human feedback)**; data annotators in Kenya, India, Pakistan, China, Philippines earn as little as $2/hour
  [3.10] **World Wide Web** | Created by **Tim Berners-Lee**; engagement-driven algorithms amplify outrage → affective polarisation, echo chambers, misinformation
[4] h: STEM and brain drain
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] **US H-1B fee** | **$100,000** (Trump administration); US IT jobs grew ~40% (2016–2024), ~25% foreign-born; rivals – China's **"K visa"**, UK considering lower fees
  [5.1] **STEM** | Term coined in **2001** by the **US National Science Foundation** (earlier SMET)
[6] table: Women-in-STEM scheme | Key detail
  [6.0] Women in STEMM (WiS) | DST – scholarships, fellowships
  [6.1] Vigyan Jyoti | 2019-20 – girls in classes 9–12
  [6.2] WISE-KIRAN | Women scientists' career-break re-entry (age 27–57)
### part 8: Health – Vaccines, Therapies and Diseases
[0] h: AdFalciVax
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] What | **India's first indigenous multi-stage malaria vaccine candidate** – **recombinant chimeric** vaccine against ***Plasmodium falciparum***; aims to stop infection and cut community transmission
  [1.1] Status | **Technology licensed to five Indian companies**; still **preclinical (TRL 4)**, awaiting approval for clinical use; est. cost ~**₹20**/dose
  [1.2] Developer | **ICMR-Regional Medical Research Centre, Bhubaneswar**, using ***Lactococcus lactis***, with ICMR-NIMR and NII, New Delhi
  [1.3] Stability | Stable **at room temperature** (over **9** months)
  [1.4] WHO-recommended malaria vaccines | **RTS,S/AS01 (Mosquirix)** and **R21/Matrix-M**
[2] h: CAR T-cell therapy
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **CAR = Chimeric Antigen Receptor** | The patient's **own T-cells** are engineered to attack cancer
  [3.1] Approved for | **Blood cancers – ALL, Non-Hodgkin Lymphoma, Multiple Myeloma**; solid tumours not yet
  [3.2] Side effects | **Cytokine Release Syndrome (CRS)**, **ICANS** (neurotoxicity)
  [3.3] Cost abroad | Over **₹3–4 crore** per patient
  [3.4] **NexCAR19** | **India's first indigenous CAR-T therapy** (blood cancer); CDSCO approval **2023**
  [3.5] **IIT Bombay + Tata Memorial Centre** | India's first cost-effective platform **"InCART"**; **DBT** supports Make-in-India CAR-T
  [3.6] Dual-targeting CAR-T (**BCMA + CD19**) | For multiple myeloma – co-developed with the Technology Development Board (**18 May 2026**)
[4] table: Therapy | First | Disease
  [4.0] **NexCAR19** | First indigenous CAR-T | Blood cancer
  [4.1] **BIRSA 101** | First indigenous CRISPR therapy | Sickle cell
[5] h: GLP-1 drugs for obesity
[6] b: **WHO for the first time endorsed GLP-1 receptor agonists for obesity**.
[7] table: Drug | Brands
  [7.0] Semaglutide | Ozempic (diabetes), Wegovy (weight loss)
  [7.1] Tirzepatide | Mounjaro, Zepbound
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] Biosimilars | **Dr. Reddy's, Cipla, Biocon** (after patent expiry ~**2025–26**)
  [8.1] Risks | **Gastroparesis, pancreatitis, thyroid cancer**
  [8.2] World (**2022**) | **1 in 8 adults obese** – **890** million adults, **160** million adolescents
[9] h: Diseases in news
[10] table: Primary Amoebic Meningoencephalitis (PAM) | Key detail
  [10.0] Cause | "Brain-eating amoeba" ***Naegleria fowleri*** – a free-living amoeba of warm freshwater and soil, tolerating up to about **46°C**
  [10.1] Route | **Water in the nose** → olfactory nerve → brain; **not communicable**; drinking contaminated water does not spread it
  [10.2] **2025** | Three cases in **Kozhikode** (death of a nine-year-old; a three-month-old infected)
  [10.3] Fatality | **Kerala about 25%** vs **about 97% globally** (early diagnosis)
[11] table: Year | PAM milestone
  [11.0] **1971** | First Indian case
  [11.1] **2016** | Kerala's first case – **Alappuzha**
  [11.2] **2024** | **14**-year-old in Kozhikode – first Indian to survive PAM (only **11** survivors globally)
[12] b: **New World screwworm** – **first US human case** confirmed **24 August 2025** (HHS and CDC) in a traveller from **El Salvador**; infestation = **myiasis**; the US eradicated it in **1966** by the **Sterile Insect Technique**; later Mexico (**1970s**), Central America (**2000s**); Florida outbreak **2017**.
[13] h: Brain and health diplomacy
[14] table: Fact | Key detail
  [14.0] **Adult neurogenesis** | Confirmed in the **dentate gyrus of the hippocampus** (neural progenitor cells), using **single-cell RNA sequencing + AI**; the cerebral cortex and hypothalamus show no consistent adult neurogenesis
  [14.1] **Hypothalamus** | Basal part of the **diencephalon (forebrain)**
  [14.2] **BHISHM cubes to the Maldives (July 2025)** | During PM Modi's state visit, India handed over **two Aarogya Maitri Health Cubes** at **Male** on **25 July 2025**; run by the Maldives Emergency Medical Service
### part 9: Genome-edited Crops, Biology and Nuclear Energy
[0] h: From GM to genome-edited crops
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **Bt cotton** | **India's only commercial GM crop**
  [1.1] **GM vs GE** | GM inserts **foreign genes** (transgenic); GE **edits the plant's own genes, no foreign DNA**
  [1.2] Technology | **CRISPR-Cas9**; claimed 17–30% higher yield, 20 days earlier maturity, drought/salinity tolerance; non-transgenic
[2] table: GE rice variety | Developer | Parent
  [2.0] DRR Rice 100 (Kamala) | ICAR-IIRR, Hyderabad | Samba Mahsuri (BPT-5204)
  [2.1] Pusa DST Rice 1 | ICAR-IARI, New Delhi | Cotton Dora Sannalu (MTU 1010)
[3] table: Edit | Benefit
  [3.0] Samba Mahsuri – Gn1a (cytokinin oxidase) edited | More spikelets, ~19% yield gain
  [3.1] MTU-1010 | Drought and salt tolerance (saline/alkaline soils)
  [3.2] Mustard (GE) | Canola-quality, low pungency, disease/pest resistance (2nd-year trials, possible release 2026)
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] Criticism | **Coalition for a GM-Free India** analysed AICRIP (2023-24) data – Pusa DST Rice 1 equal/lower yield in 12 of 20 sites; Kamala underperformed in 8 of 19; Kamala flowering 101 vs 104 days
  [4.1] Regulation | **March 2022 MoEFCC OM** – **transgene-free GE plants exempt** from GM biosafety rules; they need only **IBSC certification** (no GEAC approval)
  [4.2] **DBT guidelines (2022)** | Gene-edited crops without foreign DNA are not treated as GMOs; oversight **RCGM and GEAC**
  [4.3] Funding | National Agricultural Science Fund (~2018); ₹500 crore in Union Budget 2023-24; ICAR mapped ~178 genes in 24 field crops and 43 genes in horticultural crops
  [4.4] Tools | Cas9, Cas12a; newer **GeoCas9 and CasLambda** from the **Innovative Genomics Institute** (founded by Jennifer Doudna) – training at IARI (Feb 2025)
  [4.5] Indigenous editor | **TnpB-based mini genome editor** – team of **Kutubuddin Ali Molla, Central Rice Research Institute (CRRI), Cuttack**; 9 scientists trained abroad, 12 more scheduled
  [4.6] **Teak Defoliator Moth** (*Hyblaea puera*) | Pest of teak plantations; **KFRI's HpNPV** virus technology is an **eco-friendly** solution
[5] h: Asgard archaea (IISc study)
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] Finding | **IISc Bengaluru** (The EMBO Journal) – **Odinarchaeota** (an Asgard archaea) has **two FtsZ genes** plus a tubulin-like gene
  [6.1] **OdinFtsZ1** | Straight protofilaments; **anchors directly to membranes** (bacteria-like)
  [6.2] **OdinFtsZ2** | **Spiral ring-like structures**; **needs adaptor proteins**
  [6.3] Methods | Phylogenetics, cryo-electron microscopy
  [6.4] **Asgard archaea** | **Named after Norse gods** (Odin-, Loki-, Thorarchaeota); deep-sea sediments; **closest living relatives of eukaryotes**
  [6.5] **FtsZ** | Bacterial division protein forming the **Z-ring**; **tubulin** is the **eukaryotic** hallmark
[7] h: Nuclear energy
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] **Nuclear Energy Mission for Viksit Bharat** | Budget 2025-26 – **100 GW by 2047** from **8.8 GW**
  [8.1] **BSMR-200** | Bharat Small Modular Reactor – **200 MW** scaled-down PHWR for **captive power** (steel, cement, aluminium); SMRs proposed for AI data centres

  [5.10] **CAR-T** | Uses **T-cells**; approved only for blood cancers
  [5.11] **PAM** | **Not communicable**; drinking water does not spread it
  [5.12] Cell division proteins | **Tubulin is eukaryotic**; FtsZ is bacterial

  [5.14] **SARAL** | Makes research summaries; a communication tool, not funding

  [1.7] OSIRIS-REx | NASA | Returned **Bennu** samples in **2023**
  [1.8] OSIRIS-APEX | NASA | Renamed OSIRIS-REx; heading for asteroid **Apophis**

### part 7: Indian and Joint Space Missions 2025-26
[0] h: NISAR – NASA-ISRO Synthetic Aperture Radar
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] What | **First ISRO-NASA joint satellite** and the **world's first dual-frequency radar satellite**
  [1.1] Launch | **30 Jul 2025** by **GSLV-F16** from **Sriharikota**
  [1.2] Dual radar | **L-band SAR by NASA** + **S-band SAR by ISRO**
  [1.3] Cost / revisit | **$1.5 billion**; scans Earth **every 12 days**
  [1.4] Objectives | **Land deformation** (earthquakes, landslides, **land subsidence**), **ice sheets and glaciers**, ecosystems and **biomass**, disasters
[2] h: Missions 2025–26
[3] table: Mission | Agency / builder | Vehicle | Key detail

## topic group2 6-25 | Sep–4 Nov 2026: Environment in news - reports, indices, species, protected areas, climate negotiations
### part 0: Disasters, Cyclones and Natural Phenomena
[0] h: Nepal disaster, 26 Aug 2026
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Event | A **rock-ice collapse** near the **Nepal-China border**; material plunged about **1 km**; first read as a **4.4-magnitude quake** – **USGS** traced the signal to the collapse (energy ~**magnitude 5.2**)
  [1.1] Type | A **rock-ice collapse-triggered debris flood**, distinct from a conventional GLOF
  [1.2] Mechanism | An **ice-rock avalanche** from the northern slopes of **Mt Langtang Lirung** choked the **Lhende River (Lhende Khola)**, which feeds the **Bhotekoshi**; flash floods on the **Bhotekoshi-Trishuli** rivers
  [1.3] Toll (**NDRRMA, 8 Sep 2026**) | **1,357 dead**, **5,326** unaccounted for (it rose from a few hundred in the first days); **93,000+** need assistance; Kailash Mansarovar pilgrims missing
  [1.4] Damage | Nepal lost about **475 MW** of hydropower (**13 hydropower projects** damaged); China's **Gyirong Port (Tibet)** buried (upgraded at **$175.8 million**)
  [1.5] India | Rivers **Gandak, Kosi, Ganga**; **Bihar and UP** on alert; **Valmikinagar Barrage** watched; Maharajganj, Kushinagar; India sent **47.5 tonnes** of relief
[2] h: India's Himalayan hazard measures
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **National GLOF Risk Mitigation Programme (2025)** | **₹150 crore**; **Arunachal Pradesh, Himachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Uttarakhand**; **CWC** does GLOF risk indexing
  [3.1] **NDMA Guidelines on GLOFs** | **2020**
  [3.2] **Dam Safety Act** | **2021**
  [3.3] **National Landslide Risk Mitigation Project** | **₹1,000 crore**, **15 States**
  [3.4] **Vasundhara Lake, Chamoli** | **Uttarakhand's first real-time GLOF early warning system**; monitors water level, weather, snow depth, flow, seismic activity; **satellite communication** (no mobile network); **solar-powered**
  [3.5] Uttarakhand | Capital **Dehradun**; summer capital **Gairsain**
[4] h: Cyclones
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Indian names | **Aag, Vyom, Jhar, Probaho, Neer, Prabhanjan, Ghurni, Ambud, Jaladhi, Vega**
  [5.1] Naming body | **WMO/ESCAP Panel on Tropical Cyclones** (**13 members**, **13 names each** = **169 names**; current list adopted **April 2020**)
  [5.2] Naming rule | **RSMC New Delhi (IMD)** names North Indian Ocean cyclones; a name is assigned at **34 knots** (~**63 km/h**), **sequentially** (landfall country does not matter); a used name is not repeated
  [5.3] **Panel members (13)** | Bangladesh, India, Iran, Maldives, Myanmar, Oman, Pakistan, Qatar, Saudi Arabia, Sri Lanka, Thailand, UAE, Yemen
  [5.4] Terms | **Hurricane** (North Atlantic, NE / Central North Pacific); **typhoon** (NW Pacific); **'willy-willy'** (historical Australian term)
  [5.5] **WMO** | Est. **1950**, HQ **Geneva**
  [5.6] **IMD** | Est. **1875**, under the **Ministry of Earth Sciences** (**Dr. Jitendra Singh**)

  [7.2] **Anak Krakatau** (**6 Sep 2026**) | **Sunda Strait** (between Java and Sumatra), Indonesia | "Child of Krakatau" – rose from the caldera of the **1883** eruption (massive tsunamis); flights hit at Soekarno-Hatta and Halim Perdanakusuma airports
  [7.3] **M6.5** earthquake (**26 Oct 2026**) | **Bonin (Ogasawara) Islands**, Japan | About **800 km** SE of Japan's main islands; ~**30** volcanic islands; part of Tokyo; UNESCO Natural Heritage **2011**; near the Izu-Bonin-Mariana subduction zone

  [8.1] **Sunda Strait** | Links the **Java Sea** with the **Indian Ocean**
  [8.2] **Swiss glaciers** | Lost **5.5%** of remaining ice in **2026**, **second-highest after 2022** (GLAMOS, SCNAT); causes – **low winter snowfall + record summer heatwaves**
  [8.3] **2024 double cicada emergence (USA)** | **Brood XIII (17-year)** and **Brood XIX (13-year)** periodical cicadas emerged together – the **first such co-emergence in 221 years** (last in **1803**), across about **17** US states; next in **2245**

  [14.2] **Mercator** projection | **Gerardus Mercator, 1569**; enlarges high latitudes; useful for navigation (constant compass bearings are straight lines)
  [14.3] Scale | **Africa is ~14 times larger than Greenland**; no flat map keeps area, shape, distance and direction together

[0] h: MISHTI and mangroves

[9] h: Offshore nodule blocks near Great Nicobar

  [10.1] **Polymetallic nodules** | Nickel, cobalt, manganese, copper, REEs; ISA zones **Clarion-Clipperton** (off Mexico), **Central Indian Ocean Basin**, **Peru Basin**
  [10.2] **Polymetallic sulphides** | Copper, zinc, lead, silver, gold

  [9.5] **COFO** | FAO's highest forestry body; meets **every two years**
[10] x: Secretariats: **IUCN, WWF and Ramsar** at **Gland, Switzerland**; **CBD** at **Montreal**; **UNFCCC** at **Bonn**.
### part 5: Air Quality - Swachh Vayu Survekshan, NCAP and Stubble
[0] h: Clean Air Day and Swachh Vayu Survekshan 2026
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **International Day of Clean Air for Blue Skies** | **7 September**; designated by **UNGA in 2019**; first observed **2020**; India marks it as **Swachh Vayu Diwas**
  [1.1] **Swachh Vayu Survekshan** | Launched **2022–23** by **MoEFCC**; covers **130 NCAP cities**; **5th awards** on **7 Sep 2026**

  [2.0] Category-**1** (**10 lakh+**) | **Indore** (Jabalpur 2nd; Agra and Surat joint 3rd) | **Lucknow** (**198/200**, **₹1.5 crore**); Indore 2nd, Jabalpur 3rd
  [2.1] Category-**2** (**3–10 lakh**) | **Amravati** | **Rourkela** (**198.5**); **Guntur** joint 2nd
  [2.2] Category-**3** (under **3 lakh**) | **Dewas** (MP) (Parwanoo 2nd, Angul 3rd) | **Kalinganagar**

[5] h: NCAP progress
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] **NCAP** | Launched **2019** by MoEFCC; **130 cities** in **24 States/UTs**
  [6.1] Target | First a **20–30% PM cut by 2024** (base **2017**), revised to **40% by 2026**; tracked on the **PRANA** portal
  [6.2] Funds | Grants **2019–20 to 2025–26**: **₹16,423.55 crore**; utilised **₹11,575.54 crore** (**70.5%**)
  [6.3] PM10 (**2025–26** vs **2017–18**) | **108 of 130** cities lowered PM10; **72** cut over **20%**; **26** cut over **40%**; only **14** met the PM10 NAAQS (**60 µg/m³**)
  [6.4] PM2.5 (**2025–26**) | **65 cities** within the limit (**40 µg/m³**); **Delhi 102 µg/m³**
  [6.5] **SAMEER** app | AQI for **292+ cities**; network (Mar **2026**): **1,646 stations in 603 cities**
[7] h: AQI, GRAP and laws
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] **AQI** | **6 categories**, **8 pollutants** – **PM10, PM2.5, NO2, SO2, CO, O3, NH3, lead** (methane excluded); the **worst sub-index** decides
  [8.1] AQI bands | **Very Poor 301–400**; **Severe 401–500**
  [8.2] **PM10** | Particles **10 micrometres or smaller**
  [8.3] **GRAP** | **4 stages**, from **Poor (201–300)** to **Severe Plus (over 450)**

  [10.1] Order | **Red** most polluting → **Orange** → **Green**; **White** least polluting (practically non-polluting); **Blue** – essential environmental services such as waste treatment

### part 6: Short-lived Climate Pollutants and the Ozone Treaties
[0] h: Short-lived climate pollutants (SLCPs)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Impact | Up to **45% of present warming**; IPCC – rapid cuts can avoid up to **0.6°C by 2050**
  [1.1] Other names | **Super pollutants** / **Short-Lived Climate Forcers**
  [1.2] Warming SLCPs | **Black carbon, methane, tropospheric ozone, HFCs**; **sulphate aerosols cool**
  [1.3] **Black carbon** | A particle that absorbs radiation – outside the greenhouse gases
  [1.4] **Tropospheric ozone** | Formed (not directly emitted) from methane, NOx, VOCs in sunlight; surface to **10 km**
  [1.5] Stratospheric ozone | ~**10–50 km**
  [1.6] **Methane** | About **80 times** CO2's warming over **20 years**; second-largest contributor after CO2
  [1.7] Damage | Ozone causes loss of about **110 million tonnes** of staple crops a year; air pollution – about **7 million** premature deaths a year globally
[2] table: India | Key detail
  [2.0] Air pollution | **1.67 million deaths in 2019** (**17.8%**); loss **US$36.8 billion** (**1.36% of GDP**)
  [2.1] Methane sources (**2016**, **Third BUR**) | **Agriculture 73.96%**, Waste **14.46%**, Energy **10.62%**, Industry **0.96%**
  [2.2] Measures | **PMUY** (over **10.5 crore** LPG connections), **GOBARdhan**, **SRI**, **DSR**, **alternate wetting and drying**
  [2.3] **Global Methane Pledge** | India has stayed out; it calls farm methane "**survival emissions**"
[3] h: International instruments
[4] table: Instrument | Key detail
  [4.0] **Vienna Convention** | **1985**
  [4.1] **Montreal Protocol** | Adopted **16 Sep 1987**, in force **1 Jan 1989**; ozone-depleting substances; Ozone Day **16 September**
  [4.2] **Kigali Amendment** | Adopted **15 Oct 2016** at **Kigali, Rwanda**; in force **1 Jan 2019**; legally binding **HFC phase-down**; can avoid up to **0.5°C by 2100**
  [4.3] **Global Methane Pledge** | **COP26 Glasgow 2021**; cut **30%** from **2020** levels by **2030**; **159** countries + EC; India not a member
  [4.4] **Climate and Clean Air Coalition** | UNEP-led; India joined **2019**
[5] b: **Kigali Amendment completes 10 years (21 Oct 2026):** implemented under the **UNEP Ozone Secretariat** with the **Multilateral Fund**; **India ratified Sep 2021**; India freezes HFCs by **2028**, begins phase-down from **2032** and cuts **85% by 2047**. **HFCs do not deplete ozone**. **UNEP**: est. **1972**, HQ **Nairobi**.

### part 9: Climate Negotiations and Climate Reports
[0] h: Climate negotiations and reports
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **Paris Agreement** | Adopted at **COP21 (2015)**; in force **4 Nov 2016**; aims **well below 2°C** while pursuing **1.5°C**; **NDCs** = national climate commitments under it
  [1.1] **UNEP 17th Emissions Gap Report 2026 "The Point of Return"** (**25 Oct 2026**) | Global GHG emissions **58.4 Gt CO2e in 2025**, down only **0.1 Gt** from **2024**
  [1.2] EGR – overshoot | A **temporary overshoot of 1.5°C** is unavoidable without rapid cuts; low-overshoot pathway **1.7–1.8°C**, with about **50% chance** of returning to 1.5°C by **2100**
  [1.3] EGR – cuts needed | **18% by 2030, 45% by 2035, 69% by 2040** (vs **2025**); new NDCs (about **two-thirds** of countries) cut only **5–8% by 2030** and **11–15% by 2035**
  [1.4] **UNFCCC NDC report** (**29 Oct 2026**) | Even with full NDC implementation, emissions fall only **9% by 2035** from **2019** levels; insufficient for 1.5°C; strong cuts could limit peak warming to about **1.8°C**
[2] table: Report | Body | Key detail
  [2.0] Emissions Gap Report | **UNEP** | **17th**, "The Point of Return"
  [2.1] NDC Synthesis | **UNFCCC** | **9%** fall by **2035**
[3] h: Tuvalu (18 Oct 2026)
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] Threat | Rising sea levels may make parts uninhabitable by the end of the century
  [4.1] Identity | Formerly the **Ellice Islands**; Polynesian country between **Australia and Hawaii**; about **26 sq km** – **world's fourth-smallest country**
  [4.2] Islands | **Nine islands** (**four reef islands, five atolls**) incl. Nanumanga, Niutao, Niulakita
  [4.3] Neighbours | **Kiribati** to the north, **Fiji** to the south
  [4.4] Capital / features | **Funafuti**; **no rivers**
  [4.5] Economy | Revenue from **copra, postage stamps, fishing licences**; currency **Australian Dollar**
### part 10: New Current Affairs: 5–14 Nov 2026
[0] h: COP31 opens in Antalya (9 Nov 2026)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **COP31** | Opened **9 Nov 2026** at the expo centre in **Antalya, Türkiye** (runs to **20 Nov**); the hosts call it the "**implementation COP**"
  [1.1] President | **Murat Kurum**, Türkiye's **Minister of Environment**; Türkiye hosts the COP **for the first time**
  [1.2] Negotiations | Led by **Australia** (**Chris Bowen**, "President of negotiations")
  [1.3] **COP30** | **Belém, Brazil (2025)**
  [1.4] India's opening statement (**10 Nov 2026**) | By **Narendra Bhooshan, Secretary, MoEFCC** (Minister **Bhupender Yadav** joins in the second week)
  [1.5] India's position | Upheld **equity** and **CBDR-RC** (Common But Differentiated Responsibilities and Respective Capabilities); called the **UNFCCC and Paris Agreement** "the most inclusive platforms"; **public finance from developed countries** remains central for developing countries and adaptation
  [1.6] **35-by-35 Global Electrification Pledge** | Introduced by the **Turkish COP31 Presidency** at the **Bonn Climate Conference (June 2026)**; raise electricity's share of **global final energy consumption** from about **20–23%** to **35% by 2035**; **voluntary, non-binding**; **India had not yet decided** to join (**4 Nov**)
  [1.7] **COP28 (Dubai)** pledge | **Triple renewable capacity to 11,000 GW** and **double energy-efficiency gains (2% → 4%/yr) by 2030**
  [1.8] **UNFCCC** | Adopted **9 May 1992** (New York; opened at the Rio Earth Summit); in force **21 March 1994**; **198 Parties**; secretariat **Bonn, Germany**; **India ratified 1 November 1993**
[2] h: WMO State of the Climate 2026 (9 Nov 2026)
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] Release | By the **WMO** on the **opening day of COP31**
  [3.1] Temperature | Global mean **Jan–Sep 2026 = 1.47°C** above the **1850–1900** average; 2026 likely **among the four warmest years** (above 2025 and 2023, slightly below **2024**)
  [3.2] **El Niño** | Intensifying since **August 2026**; may become **one of the strongest on record by December 2026**
  [3.3] Greenhouse gases | **CO2 426.8 ppm (2025)**, **53% above** **278** ppm of **1750**; **methane 1,948 ppb**, **167% above** 1750 (**729** ppb)
  [3.4] Oceans | Absorb about **90% of excess heat**; 2026 on track for the **tenth consecutive year of record ocean warmth**
[4] h: Global Carbon Budget 2026 (9–10 Nov 2026)
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Report | **21st** annual **Global Carbon Budget** of the **Global Carbon Project** (**140+** scientists; preprint in *Earth System Science Data*)
  [5.1] Fossil CO2 | **38.2 billion tonnes in 2026, down 0.4%** (oil −**1.9%**, gas −**0.3%**, **coal +0.6%**)
  [5.2] Total CO2 | Incl. land use **43.3 Gt** (**42.8 Gt** in 2025); land-use emissions **5.1 Gt**
  [5.3] Countries (fossil CO2, **2026**) | **China −0.9%** (about **32%** of the world total), **USA −1.3%**, **EU −1.5%**, **India +4.4%** (above its decade average of **3.5%**)
  [5.4] **Remaining carbon budget for 1.5°C** | **About 150 Gt CO2** – **less than four years** of current emissions
  [5.5] Atmosphere | **CO2 427.7 ppm in 2026**, about **54% above** pre-industrial
[6] h: Antarctic ozone hole and cyclones
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] **2026 Antarctic ozone hole** (**07 Nov 2026**) | **7th largest** on record; forms in the **stratosphere**; the **polar vortex** isolates cold Antarctic air; **polar stratospheric clouds** provide surfaces for ozone-destroying reactions
  [7.1] **Cyclone Arnab attribution study (AP)** (**06 Nov 2026**) | **Imperial College London's Grantham Institute**, using the **Imperial College Storm Model (IRIS)**: climate change raised **economic damage by about 33%** (about **25%** attributable); **max wind +5%**, **eyewall rainfall +11%**; likelihood up **19%**; present climate **~1.3°C** above pre-industrial
  [7.2] **Arnab's landfall** | **Severe cyclonic storm** from the **Bay of Bengal**; name by **Bangladesh** ('Arnab' = **ocean / sea** in Bengali); forecast to cross near **Yanam**, it crossed on **28 Oct 2026** near **Narsapur** (about **20 km ENE of Narsapur**, **40 km SW of Yanam**); peak winds up to **202 kmph**
  [7.3] **Cyclone Murasu** (**02 Nov 2026**) | Deep depression over the **Southwest Bay of Bengal** became Cyclonic Storm **Murasu on 1 Nov 2026**, forecast to cross between **Nellore and Narsapur** (AP); rain damaged the **Polavaram Right Main Canal** lining (Eluru district)
  [7.4] Murasu – name | **India-proposed** – **Tamil** word for a **large war drum** used to announce wars, victories and royal orders (public alertness)
  [7.5] Next names | **Akvan (Iran), Kani (Maldives), Elgaman (Oman), Seil (Oman), Sahab (Pakistan)**
  [7.6] Formation | Needs **SST above 27°C** and a **Coriolis force** – so cyclones **do not form near the equator**
  [7.7] **Rayalaseema drought (Kharif 2026)** (**10 Nov 2026**) | **Kurnool** worst hit (**1.85 lakh farmers**, **1.68 lakh ha**), then Nandyal (**1.64** lakh ha) and YSR Kadapa (**1.49** lakh ha)
  [7.8] **Meteorological drought** | Rainfall **25% or more below normal**; other types – agricultural, hydrological, socio-economic
[8] h: Species, protected areas and days
[9] table: Item | Key detail
  [9.0] **International Day for Biosphere Reserves – 3 November** | Proclaimed by **UNESCO in November 2021**; first observed **3 Nov 2022** (end of **50 years of the MAB Programme**)
  [9.1] India's biosphere reserves | **18 notified**, **13** in UNESCO's **World Network of Biosphere Reserves**; India = one of **17 megadiverse countries**, **7–8% of species** on **2.4% of land**
  [9.2] ***Gegeneophis chandgadi*** (**03 Nov 2026**) | New **caecilian** (**legless amphibian**), family **Grandisoniidae**, at **Buzawade village, Chandgad taluka, Kolhapur (Maharashtra)**; caecilians live underground, mistaken for earthworms or blind snakes
  [9.3] Amphibian count | India **458 amphibian** species incl. **41 caecilians**; genus *Gegeneophis* **12** species (**11 endemic to the Western Ghats**)
  [9.4] **Cheetah strays into Rajasthan** (**03 Nov 2026**) | A **Botswana-born male** from **Kuno National Park (MP)** crossed into the **Baran forests, Rajasthan**
  [9.5] MP's cheetah sites | **Kuno** (first), **Gandhi Sagar WLS (Mandsaur)**, **Veerangana Durgavati Tiger Reserve**
  [9.6] Cheetah status | African cheetah **IUCN Vulnerable**; **Asiatic cheetah (Iran) Critically Endangered**; in **CAMI (2014)**; fastest land mammal (**~103 km/h**)
  [9.7] **Asian Openbill Storks at Punyakshetram (AP)** (**04 Nov 2026**) | Thousands arrived late (linked to **El Niño**) at **Punyakshetram village, Rajanagaram mandal, East Godavari** – a traditional nesting site; family **Ciconiidae**; feeds mainly on **freshwater snails**; **IUCN Least Concern**; **Schedule II, Wildlife (Protection) Act 1972**
  [9.8] **BOB-NOR** (**06 Nov 2026**) | **Bay of Bengal Ocean Research Network** – launched by **India, Sri Lanka, Bangladesh and the Maldives** at a meeting hosted by **ICAR-CMFRI**; climate-informed fisheries management (sea temperature, monsoon, salinity, productivity)
  [9.9] **BOBP-IGO** | Est. **2003**; members **Bangladesh, India, Maldives, Sri Lanka**
  [9.10] **BRICS Sustainable Lifestyle Week** (**1–7 Nov 2026**) | Observed in **Andhra Pradesh** under **India's BRICS Presidency (2026)**, around the **seven themes of Mission LiFE** (healthy lifestyles, saving energy, conserving water, sustainable food, reducing single-use plastic, e-waste, general waste)
  [9.11] **Green firecrackers (CSIR-NEERI)** (**04 Nov 2026**) | About **30% less particulate matter** (or **20%** less PM + **10%** less gases); types – **SWAS** (Safe Water Releaser), **STAR** (Safe Thermite Cracker), **SAFAL** (Safe Minimal Aluminium); the **Supreme Court** allowed them in Delhi for two days at Deepavali
[10] x: **COP31 President = Murat Kurum (Türkiye)**; Australia leads the negotiations. India's 2026 fossil CO2 **rises 4.4%** while the world's **falls 0.4%**.

  [1.17] E-waste **2024–25** | **13.98** lakh tonnes

  [1.19] **KMGBF** | **4** goals (**2050**), **23** targets (**2030**)
  [1.20] Cyclone panel | **13** members, **13** names each
  [1.21] **Siang Upper** project | **11,200 MW**
[2] h: Easily confused facts
[3] table: Topic | Key detail
  [3.0] Climate pollutants | **Black carbon** is a particle, outside the GHGs; **tropospheric ozone** forms in the air; **sulphate aerosols cool**; **HFCs** do not deplete ozone (Kigali controls them for warming)
  [3.1] Nepal flood | A **rock-ice collapse debris flood**; India watched the **Valmikinagar Barrage**
  [3.2] Cyclone names | Assigned **sequentially** at **34** knots; **Arnab** = Bangladesh, **Murasu** = India
  [3.3] Conferences | **COP31 = Antalya, Türkiye** (Australia leads talks); **CBD COP17 = Yerevan**; **COP18 = Quito**
  [3.4] **First GI** | **Darjeeling Tea** (**2004–05**)
  [3.5] **First Ramsar sites** | **Chilika and Keoladeo** (**1981**)
  [3.6] **First country with two ISA sulphide contracts** | **India**

[0] h: Cyclone Akvan
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Formation | **Cyclonic Storm Akvan** formed **16 Nov 2026** from a deep depression over the **south Bay of Bengal** (17:30 IST)
