### part 0: Big Cats – IBCA, Project Cheetah, Lions and Tigers
[0] h: International Big Cat Alliance (IBCA)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Launch | By **PM Narendra Modi** on **9 April 2023** at **Mysuru** – **50 years of Project Tiger**
  [1.1] Status | Union Cabinet approval **February 2024**; treaty-based organisation from **23 January 2025**; HQ / secretariat in **India**
  [1.2] **Seven big cats** | Tiger, lion, leopard, snow leopard, cheetah, jaguar, puma; India has **five**
  [1.3] Members | **24** earlier in **2026**; **Saudi Arabia joined on 22 May 2026 – 26 members and 5 observers** (observers incl. Kazakhstan, Namibia, Thailand); **Bangladesh** became the **27th** member (July **2026**)
  [1.4] **First IBCA conference** | New Delhi, **1 June 2026** – **400+** scientists, conservationists, policymakers
  [1.5] **First IBCA Summit** | Hosted by India, **1–3 June 2026, New Delhi**; ~**95 countries**
  [1.6] Outcome | **"(New) Delhi Declaration"** – **first international declaration exclusively on big cats**; landscape-based and transboundary conservation, habitat connectivity, wildlife crime, finance, science, community participation, One Health

### part 3: Air, Waste and Pollution
[0] h: Solid Waste Management Rules, 2026
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Notification | By **MoEFCC**; in force **1 April 2026**; under the **Environment Protection Act, 1986**
  [1.1] Replaces | **SWM Rules 2016** (which had **three** categories – wet, dry, domestic hazardous)
  [1.2] Scope | Also covers rural bodies; goal **zero landfill**, circular economy
  [1.3] Waste data | **620** lakh tonnes/year; ~**1.85** lakh t/day generated; **1.14** lakh t/day processed; ~**40,000** t/day landfilled
  [1.4] **Hierarchy** | **Prevention** → Reduction → Reuse → Recycling → Recovery → **Disposal (last resort)**
  [1.5] Tracking | Centralised **portal** for the full lifecycle (reporting to **CPCB**); legacy dumpsites mapped by **31 October 2026**; **District Collectors** oversee landfills; special provisions for hilly areas and islands
  [1.6] **Four-way segregation** | At source: **wet, dry, sanitary, special domestic hazardous (special-care)**
[2] table: Bin colour | Waste stream
  [2.0] Green | Wet
  [2.1] Blue | Dry
  [2.2] Red | Sanitary (wrapped separately)
  [2.3] Black | Special-care
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] **Bulk generators** | More than **100 kg/day** waste, more than **40,000 litres/day** water use, or more than **20,000 sq m** built-up area – produce ~**30%** of solid waste
  [3.1] Bulk generator duty | **Process on site** (**EBWGR** – on-site wet waste processing, portal registration, certification-based compliance)
  [3.2] **Polluter Pays** | Environmental compensation; higher landfill fees for unsegregated waste; CPCB frames guidelines, SPCBs enforce
  [3.3] Energy recovery | Waste with calorific value **≥ 1,500 kcal/kg**; industries raise **RDF** use **5% → 15%** over six years
  [3.4] Legacy dumps | **Biomining / bioremediation**; **tourist user charges** in hill / island areas
  [3.5] Related rules | Plastic Waste Rules **2016**, **E-Waste Rules 2022**, Biomedical Waste Rules **2016**
  [3.6] MoEFCC | Est. **1985**
  [3.7] **EP Act, 1986** | Enacted under **Article 253** (implementing international treaties) after the **Stockholm Declaration 1972**
  [3.8] **Subsidiarity** | Tasks at the **lowest capable level** (decentralisation)
[4] h: Varkala – UN "20 Cities Towards Zero Waste"
[5] b: **Varkala (Kerala)** is in the **first list** of "**20** Cities Towards Zero Waste" – led by the **UN Secretary-General's Advisory Board on Zero Waste** (launched **2023**); supported by **UN-Habitat** (**1978**) and **UNEP** (**1972**), both HQ **Nairobi**.
[6] h: Improved cookstoves and pollutants
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] **Improved Cookstoves (ICS)** | **Secondary aeration** → fuller combustion
  [7.1] Thermal efficiency | Traditional chulha **~10%**; ICS **38–45%**; fuel use cut up to **two-thirds**
  [7.2] Cost | Household ICS below **₹2,000**; commercial up to **₹20,000**; eligible for **carbon credits**; reduce **black carbon**
  [7.3] Limit | ICS **reduce** indoor pollution without eliminating it
  [7.4] **Photochemical smog** | **NOx + VOCs** in sunlight → **ozone, PAN**
[8] table: Pollutant type | Example | How formed
  [8.0] Primary | Carbon monoxide (CO) | Emitted directly
  [8.1] Secondary | Ozone, PAN | Formed in air
[9] h: Rare-earth mining pollutes the Mekong
[10] table: Fact | Key detail
  [10.0] Problem | Unregulated rare-earth mining in **Myanmar and Laos** – arsenic, mercury, lead, cadmium in the tributaries **Sai and Ruak** → Mekong ("world's kitchen")
  [10.1] Mekong | Longest river of **Southeast Asia**; rises in the Tibetan Plateau as the **Lancang**; flows through China, Myanmar, Laos, Thailand, Cambodia, Vietnam → South China Sea
  [10.2] **Mekong Delta (Vietnam)** | "Rice bowl" of SE Asia
  [10.3] **Tonle Sap Lake (Cambodia)** | **Seasonal flow reversal**
  [10.4] **Mekong River Commission (1995)** | **Four** full members – **Cambodia, Laos, Thailand, Vietnam**; **China, Myanmar** = dialogue partners
  [10.5] Rare earths | **17** elements (lanthanides + scandium + yttrium); **Lanthanum** – optical glass; **Dysprosium** – **NdFeB magnets** (EV motors)
  [10.6] Ores | **Monazite** has **thorium and uranium**; ionic clay deposits – in-situ leaching; **froth flotation cannot separate individual REEs**
### part 4: New Species Discovered (Mar–May 2026)
[0] h: Fauna
[1] table: Species | Type | Place / key detail
  [1.0] **191** cockroach species (ZSI, *Zootaxa*) | Insects (Blattodea) | **60%+** endemic; India's first **COI-gene DNA barcode library** for cockroaches
  [1.1] *Neoloboptera peninsularis* | Cockroach | Pune district; first DNA barcoding in Indian cockroach taxonomy
  [1.2] *Trachischium lalremsangai* | Insect-eating snake | Mizoram and Myanmar; named after Prof. **H.T. Lalremsanga** (Mizoram University); journal *Herpetozoa*
  [1.3] *Labeo kaage* ("Kaage Meenu") | Freshwater fish | Cauvery near **Shivanasamudra**, Karnataka; ICAR–NBFGR (**1983**, Lucknow); "Kaage" = crow
  [1.4] *Lutjanus arakan* | Snapper fish | First record in Indian waters – **Gopalpur-on-Sea, Odisha** (earlier only Bangladesh)
  [1.5] *Psechrus entieu, P. phenshunyu* | Spiders | **Nagaland** (ZSI); named after villages
  [1.6] *Cyrtodactylus raimonaensis* | Bent-toed gecko | Near **Raimona NP**, Kokrajhar (Assam)
  [1.7] *Megalopinus arunachalensis*, *M. mithun, M. micros* | Rove beetles | Arunachal Pradesh (Rajiv Gandhi Univ + Univ of Tübingen; *Soil Organisms*)

### part 5: Climate Negotiations and India's Updated NDC (2031–2035)
[0] h: Updated NDC – approval
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Cabinet approval | **Union Cabinet**, **25 March 2026** – India's updated **NDC (2031–2035)**, to be communicated to the **UNFCCC**
  [1.1] Submitted | **24 April 2026**
  [1.2] G20 laggards | India and **Argentina** – the **only G20 countries** without a 2035 NDC by end-2025
  [1.3] Target type | **Emissions intensity** (per unit of GDP), not absolute emissions
  [1.4] **Net zero by 2070** | Announced at **COP26, Glasgow (2021)**
[2] table: Parameter | 2022 NDC | New NDC (2035)
  [2.0] Non-fossil installed power capacity | **50%** by 2030 (achieved June 2025, five years early) | **60%** by 2035
  [2.1] Emissions intensity cut (vs 2005) | **45%** by 2030 | **47%** by 2035
  [2.2] Additional carbon sink | 2.5–3 billion t CO₂e | **3.5–4 billion t CO₂e** by 2035
  [2.3] Long-term goal | Net zero 2070 | Net zero 2070 (intensity, not absolute)
[3] b: The **2015 NDC** set an intensity cut of **33–35%**.
[4] h: Progress figures
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Emission intensity | Already cut **36%** (2005–2020)
  [5.1] Carbon sink | **2.29 billion t** created by 2021
  [5.2] Forest cover | **21.76%** (ISFR 2023)
  [5.3] Forest + tree cover | **25.17%** – against the goal of **33%**
[6] h: Emissions data (CREA / Carbon Brief)
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] CO₂ growth 2025 | Only **0.7%** – slowest since **2001** (excluding 2020); **0.5%** in the second half of 2025
  [7.1] Coal in thermal plants | Down **20%**
  [7.2] 2025 additions | **47 GW solar**, 6.3 GW wind, 4 GW hydro, 0.6 GW nuclear
  [7.3] Plans | **100 GW new coal** in 7 years; **$1 trillion** petrochemicals by 2040
  [7.4] **CREA** | Est. **2019**, HQ **Helsinki**
[8] h: CEA National Generation Adequacy Plan (2026–27 to 2035–36)
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] Basis | Part of the **20th Electric Power Survey**
  [9.1] Capacity | **520 GW (Jan 2026) → 1,121 GW by 2035–36**
  [9.2] Demand | Peak **459 GW**; energy need **3,365 BU**
  [9.3] Non-fossil | **786 GW** by 2035–36 (~70% of capacity, ~49% of generation)
  [9.4] **CEA** | Est. **1951**
  [9.5] Gas | India targets a **15% gas share** in the energy mix by 2030
[10] table: Source (2035–36) | Capacity
  [10.0] Nuclear | 22 GW
  [10.1] Gas | 20 GW
  [10.2] Biomass | 16 GW
  [10.3] Small hydro | 6 GW
[11] h: Climate treaty background
[12] table: Item | Key detail
  [12.0] **UNFCCC** | Adopted 1992 (Rio), in force 1994; CBDR; Annex I / Non-Annex I
  [12.1] **Paris Agreement** | COP21, 2015; in force 2016; well below 2°C, pursue 1.5°C; NDCs voluntary, updated every 5 years; Global Goal on Adaptation; does not explicitly mention **fossil fuels**
  [12.2] **Global Stocktake** | Every five years; reviews collective progress; no binding targets or fines
  [12.3] 1.5°C baseline | Pre-industrial (1850–1900) levels
  [12.4] **COP28** | UAE Consensus – triple renewables, double efficiency by 2030
  [12.5] **COP30** | Stressed Locally Led Adaptation (LLA)
  [12.6] UNEP Emissions Gap Report 2025 | Titled "**Off Target**"; WRI – NDCs close less than 14% of the gap
  [12.7] UNEP Adaptation Gap Report 2025 | Developing countries' annual adaptation gap **$284–339 billion** up to 2035
[13] h: First Conference on Transition Away from Fossil Fuels
[14] b: **24–29 April 2026**, **Santa Marta, Colombia**; **50+ countries**; a coalition to phase down fossil fuels.
[15] b: **Tribal Ecological Communitarianism (TEC):** a socio-economic framework of **collective ownership, cooperative labour and equitable resource distribution** with **"ecological embeddedness"** – a sustainable alternative to profit-driven models; supported by **FRA (Gram Sabhas), PESA** and **Van Dhan Yojana**.
### part 6: Species Conservation and Human-wildlife Conflict
[0] h: Great Indian Bustard – jumpstart method
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] GIB chick | Hatched **26 March 2026** at **Naiya grassland, Kutch (Gujarat)** – first successful **jumpstart** use in Gujarat; flew ~18 April, then missing 4–5 days (likely predation); not geotagged
  [1.1] **Jumpstart** | A wild female's **infertile egg is replaced with a fertile egg** from a **Rajasthan captive centre**; the wild mother raises the chick
  [1.2] Gujarat population | Only **three** surviving wild female GIBs
  [1.3] **Jerdon's Courser** | Endemic to the **Eastern Ghats of Andhra Pradesh**; rediscovered **1986**
[2] table: Species | IUCN status
  [2.0] White-bellied Heron | Critically Endangered
  [2.1] Himalayan Quail | Critically Endangered
  [2.2] Asiatic Lion | Endangered
[3] h: Honey bee attacks and NBHM
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] Bee attacks | Deaths in UP, Gujarat, Maharashtra; **Uttar Pradesh** considering bee attacks as a **state-notified disaster**
  [4.1] ***Apis dorsata*** (Giant Rock Bee) | Open hives on cliffs/forts; up to **60,000 bees**; chases intruders at **~35 km/h**; more aggressive in summer; dies after a single sting; swatting releases pheromones; risk of anaphylaxis
  [4.2] **National Beekeeping and Honey Mission** | Central Sector Scheme – "**Sweet Revolution**"; **₹500 crore**, FY 2020–21 to 2025–26; under Atmanirbhar Bharat; by the **National Bee Board** (**Ministry of Agriculture**); 3 Mini Missions
  [4.3] **Madhukranti portal** | Registration and traceability
  [4.4] Honey exports FY 2023–24 | **1.07 lakh MT** (USD 177.55 million); India **second-largest exporter** (from 9th in 2020)
  [4.5] **Waggle dance** | Decoded by **Karl von Frisch** (Nobel)
[5] h: Lantana to biochar – Amrabad Tiger Reserve
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] Pilot | **Amrabad TR** converts invasive **Lantana camara** into **biocharcoal ("black gold")** – burned under soil in low oxygen; initial ~3 kg; ~₹7,000/tonne; livelihoods through **JFMCs**
  [6.1] Origin | Native to **Central and South America**; spread in the 17th century as an ornamental
  [6.2] Status | Among IUCN's **world's worst invasive species**; **toxic to livestock**
  [6.3] Spread | **Allelopathy**, bird/monkey seed dispersal, deep roots, **resin-coated leaves**
[7] b: **Allelopathy:** a plant releases chemicals that **suppress the growth of other plants** nearby.
[8] h: Enforcement and monitoring
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] **Operation Thunder** | Wildlife-trafficking crackdown in **134 countries** – **INTERPOL (1923, Lyon)** + **WCO (1952, Brussels)**
  [9.1] **Anavaran** portal | **FSI**, since **January 2024** – satellite + machine-learning detection of forest loss; alerts to states **every 15 days**
  [9.2] **FSI** | **1981**, Dehradun
[10] h: Species in news
[11] table: Fact | Key detail
  [11.0] **Ganges soft-shell turtle** (*Nilssonia gangetica*) | India's **first satellite-tagged** one released **15 May 2026** in **Kaziranga NP and TR, Assam**; **Endangered**; **Schedule I**, WPA 1972
  [11.1] **Himalayan Salamander** (*Tylototriton himalayanus*) | Rare amphibian, one of **two salamander species in India** (other: Manipuri Newt); endemic to the **eastern Himalaya** (India, eastern Nepal, Bhutan); in India in **Darjeeling**
### part 7: Extreme Weather and Climate Terms
[0] h: Heatwave (IMD criteria)
[1] table: Criterion | Value
  [1.0] Minimum maximum temperature | 40°C plains, 30°C hills
  [1.1] Heat wave (departure) | 4.5–6.4°C above normal
  [1.2] Severe heat wave (departure) | Over 6.4°C
  [1.3] Heat wave (absolute) | 45°C
  [1.4] Severe heat wave (absolute) | 47°C
  [1.5] **Wet-bulb temperature** | Above **35°C** sustained can be fatal
  [1.6] **Feels-like (apparent) temperature** | Temperature + humidity + wind
[2] h: Western Disturbance and ENSO
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] IMD forecast | Fresh **Western Disturbance** over North India on **11–13 May 2026**
  [3.1] Western Disturbance | **East-moving** rain system from beyond **Afghanistan/Iran**; moisture from the **Mediterranean**, Black, Caspian and Arabian seas
  [3.2] Movement and effect | Moves with the **subtropical westerly jet**; brings **winter rain/snow to north-west India**; helps **wheat**
  [3.3] **US Climate Prediction Center (April 2026)** | ENSO-neutral now; El Niño likely in **May–July 2026** – **61%** chance, persisting to end-2026
  [3.4] **ECMWF** | **Super El Niño** likely in 2026, from June–August to year-end
  [3.5] Super El Niño | SST rise **above 2.5°C**; past ones **1982–83, 1997–98, 2015–16**
  [3.6] Effects | May **weaken the SW monsoon**, reduce Atlantic hurricanes, raise Pacific activity
[4] h: Kashmir's "death of winter"
[5] table: Indicator | Value
  [5.0] Below-normal snowfall | **Seven consecutive winters** (every winter since 2019–20)
  [5.1] Winter precipitation (Dec–Feb 2025–26) | 100.6 mm vs normal 284.9 mm – 65% deficit
  [5.2] February precipitation | 14.2 mm vs 130.4 mm – ~89% deficit
  [5.3] Impacts | **Apples, cherries, walnuts** lack **chilling hours**; pests (aphids, mites); winter tourism (Gulmarg, Pahalgam) hit
[6] h: Earthquake lights (EQL)
[7] b: Luminous atmospheric phenomena **before or during earthquakes**; cause – **ionisation of air** by electrical charges generated under **tectonic stress** in rocks.

[4] h: Other protected areas and sites

  [5.5] **Amchang WLS** | **Eastern fringe of Guwahati, Assam**, bounded north by the **Brahmaputra**; extension of Meghalaya's **Khasi and Jaintia Hills**; WLS in **2004**; Gauhati HC questioned tree felling for the **Guwahati Ring Road**
  [5.6] **Asola Bhatti WLS** | Invasive **Prosopis juliflora** has colonised **63.48%** of the sanctuary
  [5.7] **Laokhowa–Burhachapori WLS** (Assam) | Twin protected ecosystem on the **Brahmaputra's southern bank**, connecting **Kaziranga and Orang National Parks**; fringe women turned **water hyacinth** into wealth

[0] h: River Basin Management (RBM) Scheme

  [1.2] Approach | Whole river system as **one hydrological unit**; basins incl. Brahmaputra, Barak, Teesta, Indus
  [1.3] Works | Flood/erosion control in the NE (Majuli Island); springshed management in the hills

### part 1: Pollution and Waste
[0] h: Methane from landfills
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Satellite data | Methane from Indian landfills far higher than official estimates; globally up to **1.8 times** model estimates
  [1.1] India | **Third-largest methane emitter** (after **China, USA**); **31 million tonnes/year** (~9% of global)
  [1.2] Waste sector | ~15% of India's methane; 3rd BUR: 409 million t CO₂e
  [1.3] CH₄ | Simplest alkane; 80–95% of natural gas; lighter than air
  [1.4] GWP | **84 times** CO₂ over 20 years; 28–34 times over 100 years
  [1.5] Platforms | ISRO (2023 study), CarbonMapper–Tanager, SRON, ClimateTRACE, WasteMap
  [1.6] Capture example | **Indore's Bio-CNG plant** under **GOBARdhan**
[2] table: City | Landfill hotspot
  [2.0] Ahmedabad | Pirana
  [2.1] Mumbai | Deonar, Kanjurmarg (10 times model estimates)
  [2.2] Delhi | Ghazipur, Bhalswa
  [2.3] Hyderabad | Jawaharnagar
  [2.4] Kolkata | Dhapa
[3] h: Indoor air pollution
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] Indoor vs outdoor | Indoor air can be **2.5 times** more polluted (up to 100 times); linked to 4.1% of global deaths
  [4.1] Standards | **India has no uniform Indoor Air Quality (IAQ) protocol**
  [4.2] WHO household air pollution (HAP) | **3.2 million deaths/year**; 86 million healthy life years lost (2019)
  [4.3] **COPD** | Progressive, irreversible; leading cause tobacco smoking; confirmed by **spirometry**
  [4.4] **PMUY** | LPG for clean cooking; traditional stoves emit **black carbon and methane** (SLCPs)
[5] table: Cause of HAP deaths | Share
  [5.0] Ischaemic heart disease | 32%
  [5.1] Stroke | 23%
  [5.2] Lower respiratory infections | 21%
  [5.3] COPD | 19%
[6] table: Air-purifying plant | Scientific name
  [6.0] Areca Palm | *Dypsis lutescens*
  [6.1] Weeping Fig | *Ficus benjamina*
  [6.2] Spider Plant | *Chlorophytum comosum*
  [6.3] Peace Lily | *Spathiphyllum wallisii*
  [6.4] Bamboo Palm | *Chamaedorea seifrizii*
[7] h: Bioremediation and phosphorus
[8] b: **Bioremediation**: bacteria, fungi, algae and plants break pollutants into non-toxic by-products (water, CO₂, organic acids); GM microbes can be engineered for it.
[9] table: Type | Meaning | Example
  [9.0] In situ | Clean on the spot | Oil-eating bacteria sprayed on an ocean spill
  [9.1] Ex situ | Remove, treat, return | Soil washing of pesticide farmland
[10] x: **Heavy metals** (cadmium, lead) **cannot be completely degraded** – only transformed or immobilised.
[11] table: Fact | Key detail
  [11.0] India | **No unified national bioremediation standards**; no biosafety rules for GM microbes
  [11.1] **China** | Uses GM bacteria under its soil pollution law
  [11.2] **Japan** | Integrates bioremediation into city waste systems
  [11.3] **Phosphorus** | Symbol P, atomic number **15**, Group 15; white (toxic), red (stable); largest use – fertilisers
  [11.4] Excess phosphorus | → **eutrophication** (US farmlands)
### part 2: Elephants, Tigers and Big Animals
[0] h: Elephants
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Herds | **Matriarchal**
  [1.1] Gestation | **18–22 months** (longest among mammals)
[2] h: "Elephant emergency" in Central India (Feb 2026)
[3] table: Fact | Key detail
  [3.0] Attacks | **Jharkhand, West Bengal, Odisha**; **less than 8%** of elephants cause ~**50%** of conflict casualties
  [3.1] Causes | Droughts 1982–83, 1986–87 (El Niño); mining in Singhbhum, Keonjhar, Sundargarh; **Rengali Dam (1985)**; linear infrastructure
  [3.2] Spread – West Bengal | From Dalma (1986) – ~200 in Jangalmahal
  [3.3] Spread – other states | Chhattisgarh – Surguja, Surajpur, Hasdeo Arand; MP – Bandhavgarh, Sanjay-Dubri; Maharashtra – Gadchiroli, Tadoba-Andhari
[4] h: Tigers
[5] b: **NTCA**: **166 tiger deaths in 2025** (vs 126 in 2024); 31 cubs.
[6] table: State | Deaths (2025)
  [6.0] Madhya Pradesh ("Tiger State") | 55
  [6.1] Maharashtra | 38
  [6.2] Kerala | 13
  [6.3] Assam | 12
[7] table: Fact | Key detail
  [7.0] **NTCA** | Est. **2006** under the 2006 amendment of **WPA 1972**
  [7.1] **Project Tiger** | **1973**; on its 50th anniversary the Centre formed **four expert working groups** to modernise it
  [7.2] Tiger reserves | **58** (Sep 2026)
  [7.3] **M-STrIPES** | Monitoring System for Tigers: Intensive Protection and Ecological Status – launched **2010** by NTCA with WII
  [7.4] **Anamalai TR (Tamil Nadu)** | Tiger census with M-STrIPES; south of the Palakkad Gap; neighbours Parambikulam TR, Chinnar WLS, Eravikulam NP; tribes Kadar, Muduvar, Malasar
  [7.5] **Ratapani TR** | **Raisen and Sehore**, MP; Vindhyas, north of the Narmada; **Kolar River** western boundary – to be renamed after **Dr. Vishnu Shridhar Wakankar**, discoverer of the **Bhimbetka** rock shelters (Padma Shri 1975)
  [7.6] **Melghat TR** | **BNHS** released **15 critically endangered Indian vultures**; Gavilgarh Hills (Satpura offshoot); **first tiger reserve in Maharashtra**; tribes **Korku (largest)**, Gawli, Gond; stronghold of the **forest owlet**
[8] h: Kaziranga elevated corridor
[9] b: PM Narendra Modi inaugurated a **34.5 km** elevated corridor in **Kaziranga NP** – between the **Brahmaputra and Karbi (Mikir) Hills**; UNESCO WHS 1985; NP 1974; tiger reserve; **world's largest population of one-horned rhinoceros**; Gangetic river dolphin.
[10] h: Gharials, dolphins, dugongs
[11] table: Fact | Key detail
  [11.0] **Gandak River** (West Champaran, Bihar) | **India's second-largest gharial habitat** – adults 54 (2015) → **400+ (2025)**; 1,000+ incl. juveniles; Wildlife Trust of India (1998) + Bihar forest department
  [11.1] Largest gharial habitat | **Chambal (2,400+)**
  [11.2] *Gavialis gangeticus* | **Critically Endangered**; the Gandak joins the Ganga on its left bank
  [11.3] **Odisha Dolphin Census 2026** | **765** – highest in five years
  [11.4] **Dugong** | Keystone seagrass grazer; the **EAC** asked Tamil Nadu to revise the International Dugong Conservation Centre at **Manora, Thanjavur**

### part 4: Protected Areas, Eco-sensitive Zones and Court Rulings
[0] h: Protected areas and ESZs
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] **Aralam Butterfly Sanctuary** (Kannur) | Renamed from Aralam WLS – **Kerala's first** butterfly sanctuary; notified under **Section 18(1), WPA 1972**; ~55 sq km; river Cheenkanni; Slender Loris; borders Brahmagiri WLS (Karnataka) and Kottiyoor WLS; **266** of Kerala's 327 butterfly species
  [1.1] **Mud puddling** | Butterflies absorb **minerals and salts** (not nectar)
  [1.2] **Kumbhalgarh WLS** | Rajasthan, Aravallis; est. **1971**; ESZ **0 to 1 km**
  [1.3] **Parvati–Arga Bird Sanctuary** (UP) | ESZ notified (wetland ecosystem)
  [1.4] **Bannerghatta NP** | **CEC (Jan 2026)** – restore the ESZ to the **2016 extent**, cancel the smaller 2020 notification; **Anekal hills**, ~22 km south of Bengaluru; reserved forest 1970, NP 1974
  [1.5] **Pangolakha WLS** | East Sikkim, ~13,000 ft, near the India–China border – forest fire; Pangolakha range part of the **Sikkim–Bhutan boundary**
  [1.6] Law | ESZs are notified under the **EP Act, 1986**
[2] b: **Forest fires:** **Valley of Flowers NP** (Chamoli, Uttarakhand; WHS with Nanda Devi NP); **Lohit Valley** (Arunachal) – IAF aerial water bombing; the **Lohit is a tributary of the Brahmaputra**.
[3] h: Great Nicobar Island Project
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] NGT ruling | **16 Feb 2026** – 6-member bench under Chairperson **Justice Prakash Shrivastava** **upheld the environmental clearance**, citing adequate safeguards, and demanded strict compliance; ₹80,000–81,000 crore
  [4.1] Origin | **Conceived by NITI Aayog**; launched **2021**; cost first put at **₹72,000 crore**
  [4.2] Components | **International Container Transshipment Terminal at Galathea Bay**, greenfield (dual-use) international airport, township, gas and solar plant; development area 166 sq km
  [4.3] Location and aim | Near the **Six Degree Channel** and **Malacca Strait**; cut dependence on **Colombo, Singapore, Port Klang**
  [4.4] Concerns | ~**130 sq km rainforest** (lakhs of trees felled); ~**700 ha** allegedly in **ICRZ 2019** prohibited zones; **Giant Leatherback Turtle** nesting at Galathea Bay; coral reefs; high seismic/tsunami zone (2004)
  [4.5] Tribes | **Shompen (PVTG) and Nicobarese**; Shompen Policy 2015; NCST under Article 338A(9)
[5] h: Aravalli protection
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] **Supreme Court (Nov 2025)** | Uniform definition – only hills **above 100 m** are Aravallis; **paused fresh mining leases** in Delhi, Haryana, Rajasthan, Gujarat; **Management Plan for Sustainable Mining** for the **entire range**; no total ban (total bans fuel illegal mining)
  [6.1] **29 December 2025** | The 20 November definition order **stayed**; bench CJI **Surya Kant**, Joymalya Bagchi, Vipul M. Pancholi; refused the Haryana jungle safari DPR until the Aravalli is defined
  [6.2] Expert committee | Chaired by **Kanchan Devi** – to define the range's limits; report by **31 Aug 2026**
  [6.3] The range | **Nearly two billion years old** – India's oldest range; ~670–692 km from Gujarat to Delhi
  [6.4] Highest peak | **Guru Shikhar (1,722 m), Mount Abu, Rajasthan**
  [6.5] Law | Protection is part of **Article 21** case law
  [6.6] Restoration study | Sankal Foundation – pilot in **four villages around Gurugram**; ~**43%** of households depend on Aravalli forests
  [6.7] UNCCD goal | Restore 26 million ha by 2030
[7] table: Year | Aravalli milestone
  [7.0] 2009 | SC mining ban in Gurugram, Faridabad, Mewat
  [7.1] May 2024 | Halt on new mining leases
  [7.2] 2025 | Aravalli Green Wall – 5-km buffer in 29 districts of four states
  [7.3] Nov 2025 | Uniform 100-m definition
  [7.4] Dec 2025 | Definition order stayed
[8] h: Other rulings
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] **Pennaiyar dispute** | Tamil Nadu sued Karnataka under **Article 131**; SC told the Centre to set up a **tribunal within one month** (ISRWD Act 1956; Article 262)
  [9.1] **Rajaji NP** | Uttarakhand; named after C. Rajagopalachari; TR 2015 – SC allowed a road, no commercial vehicles
  [9.2] **Suav river** | NGT ordered UP to reclassify it from "nallah" to river; **tributary of the Rapti** (Balrampur)
  [9.3] **SCNBWL** | Standing Committee of the National Board for Wildlife – **draft guidelines** to regulate **religious structures inside wildlife sanctuaries**
### part 5: Climate Negotiations – Paris Agreement at 10
[0] h: Paris Agreement
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] 10 years | Completed in **November 2025**; re-evaluated after the **US exit (early 2025)** and **COP30 (Belém, Brazil)**
  [1.1] Adopted | **2015, COP21 Paris**, under the UNFCCC
  [1.2] Nature | Legally binding framework with **voluntary national targets**; **NDCs every 5 years**
  [1.3] **Global Stocktake** | Every 5 years – first at **COP28 (2023)**
  [1.4] Finance | USD 100 billion/yr till 2025; **NCQG (COP29, 2024)** – at least **USD 300 billion/yr by 2035**
  [1.5] Global South demand | USD 1.3 trillion (at least USD 600 billion as grants)
  [1.6] **China** | Peak before 2030, **net zero 2060**
[2] table: Feature | Kyoto Protocol | Paris Agreement
  [2.0] Approach | Top-down | Bottom-up
  [2.1] Who has targets | Only developed nations | All parties (NDCs)
[3] h: CITES CoP20 (Samarkand, Uzbekistan, 24 Nov – 5 Dec 2025)
[4] b: The **first CITES CoP in Central Asia**; **77 species** added to the CITES Appendices (incl. sharks and iguanas); proposals to weaken protection rejected – **rhino horn and ivory trade bans stay**.
[5] h: Himalayan climate finance – ICIMOD
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] India's need | ~**USD 102 billion/yr** for Himalayan adaptation and mitigation (based on India's First Determination Report 2020 to the UNFCCC)
  [6.1] **HKH** region | Needs **USD 768.7 billion/yr**; India + China (USD 605 billion) = 92%
  [6.2] HKH | **Eight countries** – the "**Third Pole**"
[7] h: International Year 2026
[8] b: The UN declared **2026** the **International Year of Rangelands and Pastoralists**; grasslands cover ~**40%** of Earth's land; their carbon is stored mainly **in the soil** (forests – above ground).
### part 6: Wetlands and Ramsar Sites
[0] h: World Wetlands Day 2026
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Date | **2 February 2026**
  [1.1] Theme | **"Wetlands and traditional knowledge: Celebrating cultural heritage"**
  [1.2] Traditional systems | **Kulam** tank cascades (Tamil Nadu); **kenis** – traditional shallow wells, **200+ years old** (**Wayanad, Kerala**); traditional fishing in **Srikakulam (AP)**
[2] h: Wetlands in news
[3] table: Wetland | Key detail
  [3.0] Kole Wetlands | Kerala – below-sea-level rice; USD 54 million/year
  [3.1] East Kolkata Wetlands | Save ₹4,680 million/year by treating sewage
  [3.2] Bhitarkanika | Mangroves reduced damage in Cyclone Dana (2024)
  [3.3] Tso Kar | Ladakh – high-altitude Ramsar site
  [3.4] Renuka Lake | Himachal – protected by religious norms
  [3.5] Harike | Migratory birds down 12% (2021–23)

[4] h: Schemes and measures
[5] table: Measure | Key detail
  [5.0] **Amrit Dharohar (2023)** | Livelihood + eco-tourism; first-phase sites Sultanpur, Sirpur, Yashwant Sagar, Bhitarkanika, Chilika; training ALP and Paryatan Navik Certificate
  [5.1] **Wetland City Accreditation** | **Indore and Udaipur** – India's **first** accredited Wetland Cities (**January 2025**)
  [5.2] **Mission Sahbhagita / Wetland Mitras** | 2 million sensitised; 80,000 wetlands surveyed; 6,200 health cards; 18,000 Wetland Mitras
  [5.3] **Green Credit Programme** | Credits for mangrove restoration since 2023
  [5.4] SC ground-truthing | Wetlands below 2.25 ha; 1,70,000 ground-truthed, 1,00,000 demarcated; 2017 SC penalty ₹50,000 for no inventories
  [5.5] **NPCA** | Revised guidelines – catchment-scale management
  [5.6] **Wetlands (Conservation and Management) Rules, 2010** | Linked to coverage of **catchment areas**
  [5.7] Ramsar Convention | Does not make protection of all wetlands mandatory
[6] b: Blue carbon: mangroves and peatlands sequester **10–50 times faster** than forests. India lost nearly **one-third** of its wetlands in three decades.
[7] x: **Wetlands International** is a **non-governmental organisation**, not an intergovernmental body.
### part 7: Cyclones and Extreme Weather
[0] h: Cyclone Ditwah (late Nov – Dec 2025)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Formed over | **Bay of Bengal**; name given by **Yemen**
  [1.1] Impact | Hit **Sri Lanka** – **600+ deaths**; India ran **Operation Sagar Bandhu**
  [1.2] **Red Alert (30 November 2025)** | **Tirupati, Prakasam and Nellore** (AP); fishermen barred from sea till 2 December
  [1.3] Track | Moved **parallel to the Tamil Nadu–Puducherry coast** – it **did not make landfall near Puducherry**; weakened into a depression
[2] h: Cyclone Gezani
[3] b: Struck **Madagascar** (Feb 2026) – landfall on the east coast at the port city **Toamasina**; at least 36 deaths.
[4] h: Bomb cyclone (northern US, 29 December 2025)
[5] table: Fact | Key detail
  [5.0] Nature | Intense **mid-latitude (extra-tropical)** storm
  [5.1] **Bombogenesis** | Central pressure drops by **at least 24 millibars in 24 hours**
  [5.2] Formation | **Cold Arctic/Canadian air** meets warm moist air
  [5.3] Season | Any season, **most frequent in fall and winter**
  [5.4] Regions | Mostly the western North Atlantic (Gulf Stream); also NW Pacific, occasionally the Mediterranean
  [5.5] Hazards | Winds 80 kmph+, blizzards, lakeshore flooding, **thundersnow**
[6] table: Aspect | Bomb cyclone | Hurricane
  [6.0] Type | Mid-latitude (extra-tropical) | Tropical cyclone
  [6.1] Energy | Temperature contrast | Warm ocean waters
  [6.2] Season | Fall and winter | Summer, early autumn
  [6.3] Core | Cold-core | Warm-core
[7] b: **Eye of a tropical cyclone:** calm, with light winds and clear skies; the **strongest winds are in the eyewall** around the eye.
[8] x: The **eye of a tropical cyclone is warmer**, not colder, than its surroundings.
[9] h: Urban heat islands and birds
[10] table: Fact | Key detail
  [10.0] Effect | Urban birds are early victims of **UHI** – dehydration, collapse in heatwaves
  [10.1] UHI intensity in India | **2–10°C**; surface hotspots 6–8°C hotter; tree-rich areas 1–3°C cooler (Delhi Ridge, Lodhi Gardens)
  [10.2] Ahmedabad (May 2025) | Jivdaya Charitable Trust treated ~3,800 birds
  [10.3] Links | National Mission on Sustainable Habitat; **SDG 11** (Sustainable Cities), **SDG 15** (Life on Land)
### part 8: Species Conservation and Community Efforts
[0] h: Species and community efforts
[1] table: Species / effort | Key detail
  [1.0] **Lion-tailed macaque** (*Macaca silenus*, **Endangered**) | **CCMB Hyderabad** 40-year study in the **Anamalai Hills** (Dr Govindaswamy Umapathy): ~800 macaques in 37 groups; fragmented habitats at higher risk
  [1.1] **Galaxy frog** (*Melanobatrachus indicus*) | Seven presumed dead from **photo tourism** disturbance, Western Ghats (Kerala); lives **under rotten logs**; ~2–3.5 cm; IUCN Vulnerable; **no vocal calls**
  [1.2] **Pangolins** | **United Sangtam Likhum Pumji** (Sangtam Naga – Kiphire, Tuensang) resolved to protect them
  [1.3] Pangolin status | **Indian pangolin – Endangered**; **Chinese pangolin – Critically Endangered**; both **Schedule I** and **CITES Appendix I**
  [1.4] **Goa Bird Atlas** | Goa – **second state after Kerala** to publish a state bird atlas; released at the 9th Bird Festival, **Valpoi**; theme "Majestic Mhadei"; ~370 sq km, ~1,400 km transects
  [1.5] **Indian Skimmer** (*Rynchops albicollis*, Endangered, Schedule I) | **BNHS (1883) + NMCG** project launched by Jal Shakti Minister **C. R. Patil** in Dehradun; protects **river sandbars** (nesting)
  [1.6] **JALAJ Livelihood Centres** | **25** opened (target **75**) – joint initiative of **Namami Gange** (2014) and **WII** (1982, Dehradun)
  [1.7] State microbe | **Kerala** – **first state to declare a state microbe**: ***Bacillus subtilis*** (Gram-positive, rod-shaped, spore-forming probiotic, facultative aerobe, 25–35°C)
  [1.8] **Nagar Van Yojana** (MoEFCC) | **Six urban forests** sanctioned in **Telangana**
### part 9: Cryosphere, GLOFs and Disaster Management
[0] h: GLOF study (Jan 2026)
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Journal | **npj Natural Hazards** – GLOF risks in the **Himalaya–Karakoram** poorly assessed; ~**1 million people** downstream at risk
  [1.1] **GLOF** | Sudden rush of water and debris when the natural dam holding a glacial lake breaks
  [1.2] Most events | **Karakoram**
  [1.3] Since 1990 | Glacial lakes +53% in number, area +51%, volume +48%
  [1.4] Driver | **Elevation-dependent warming**; lake-terminating glaciers retreat faster
  [1.5] Sikkim 2023 GLOF | 55 deaths
  [1.6] **Ghepang Ghat Glacial Lake** | Lahaul and Spiti, HP, ~4,068 m – grew **178%** (36.49 ha in 1989 → 101.30 ha in 2022), per **ISRO**
[2] table: Country | GLOF events (total 388)
  [2.0] Pakistan | 131
  [2.1] India | 59
  [2.2] Nepal | 54
[3] h: Thwaites ("Doomsday") Glacier
[4] b: **Thwaites Glacier** (**West Antarctica**, the **'Doomsday Glacier'**): full collapse → ~**65 cm** sea-level rise; fracture length **165 km (2002) → 335 km (2022)**; two-stage fracture; Eastern Ice Shelf pinned to a seabed ridge.
[5] h: NDMA – Disaster Victim Identification (DVI)
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] Guidelines | **India's first-ever** national guidelines and SOPs for DVI, by **NDMA**
  [6.1] Triggers | **Air India crash (Ahmedabad)**, **Sangareddy chemical explosion**, Uttarakhand flash floods, Delhi car bomb blast
  [6.2] **Four stages** | Systematic recovery → post-mortem data → ante-mortem data → **reconciliation** (final)
  [6.3] Records | **National Dental Data Registry** (teeth and jaws survive extreme conditions); "humanitarian forensics"; INTERPOL best practices
[7] x: **Mass autopsies are discouraged**, not mandated.
[8] h: Kerala – tidal flooding a state disaster
[9] b: **Kerala** – **first** state to declare **tidal flooding** (sunny-day / king-tide / nuisance flooding) a **state-specific disaster**; worse at full and new moon; relief via the **SDRF (DM Act, 2005)**; coastline ~590 km.
### part 10: Rivers, Dams, Lakes and Ports
[0] h: Rivers, dams, lakes and ports
[1] table: Project / place | Key detail
  [1.0] **Sawalkot** | On the **Chenab**, **Ramban** district, J&K (after India put the Indus Waters Treaty in abeyance post-Pahalgam)
  [1.1] **Shahpur Kandi** | On the **Ravi**, **Pathankot**; national project **2008**; costs **80% Punjab, 20% Centre**
  [1.2] **Indus Waters Treaty** | 19 September 1960, World Bank: **Ravi, Beas, Sutlej to India**; Indus, Jhelum, Chenab to Pakistan
  [1.3] **Bargi Dam** | MP, on the **Narmada**; 1974–90; among the first completed on the Narmada – **NDSA** show-cause notice to the **Narmada Valley Development Authority**
  [1.4] NDSA | Under the **Dam Safety Act, 2021**
  [1.5] **Tehri Dam (260 m)** | **Tallest dam in India**; on the Bhagirathi; THDC India
  [1.6] **Dal Lake** | Drains into the **Jhelum** (rises at **Verinag**)
  [1.7] **Futala (Telangkhedi) Lake, Nagpur** | Man-made, built under the **Bhonsles** (18th century)
  [1.8] **Vadhavan Port** (Palghar) | ~20,000 protested; ~**140 km north of Mumbai**, natural depth ~**20 m**; ₹76,220 crore; JNPA + Maharashtra Maritime Board
  [1.9] Palghar district | **Carved out of Thane in 2014**

## topic group2 6-28 | Overview and data: Environment in news - reports, indices, species, protected areas, climate negotiations
### part 0: Green Firsts, Campaigns and Events in News
[0] h: Firsts
[1] table: First | Key detail
  [1.0] First city to run on 100% renewable energy in the daytime | **Diu** Smart City (solar) – also the first UT to run 100% on solar
  [1.1] First railway station with ISO 14001:2015 (environment management) | **Guwahati** – in **2019**, acting on **NGT** directions, for passenger amenities in a clean and green environment; the certificate is ISO's
  [1.2] India's first Owl Festival | **Pingori, near Pune** (Purandar taluka, Maharashtra), by the **Ela Foundation**, from **29 Nov 2018** – to dispel superstitions about owls
  [1.3] World's first fully solar-powered airport | Cochin International Airport
  [1.4] First treaty-based IGO headquartered in India | International Solar Alliance (first summit New Delhi, 2018)
  [1.5] First carbon-neutral airport in Asia-Pacific | **Indira Gandhi International Airport, Delhi** (ACI Airport Carbon Accreditation Level 3+ 'Neutrality', 2016); later Asia-Pacific's first **Level 4+**

[3] h: Natural vs man-made disasters

  [1.2] Firsts and last | **Australia in the Top 10 for the first time**; **Chad last (145th)**
  [1.3] India | **131st** – parity **64.5%**; **same rank as 2025** (131st of 148, up from 129th in 2024; Political Empowerment the weakest then too); up **4.3 points** since 2006; **67th in Political Empowerment**
  [1.4] World | Closed **69.2%** of the gap; full parity in about **120 years**; **no country at full parity**

[0] h: Climate indices at COP31
[1] table: Fact | Key detail

  [1.2] CCPI since | **2005**

  [1.4] **CCPI 2027 ranks** | **1st–3rd left vacant** (no country good enough); **Denmark 4th** (best); **UK 7th** – the **only G20** member rated 'High'; Morocco 8th, Chile 9th; EU 19th; Pakistan 26th; **China 56th**; host **Türkiye 53rd**
  [1.5] CCPI 2027 bottom | Saudi Arabia 65th, Russia 66th, **USA 67th**, **Iran 68th (last)**
  [1.6] **CCPI 2026** | Released at **COP30, Belém** (Nov 2025) by **Germanwatch, NewClimate Institute and Climate Action Network**; **63 countries + EU**
  [1.7] CCPI 2026 – India | **Fell 13 places to 23rd** (score **61.31**; 'High' → **'Medium'** performer) – **no coal-exit timeline**, new coal block auctions
  [1.8] CCPI 2026 ranks | **Ranks 1–3 vacant** (no country 'very high'); **Denmark 4th** (80.52), UK 5th, Morocco 6th; **Saudi Arabia last (67th)**
  [1.9] Performance vs impact | CCPI ranks climate **performance**; Germanwatch's **Climate Risk Index** ranks climate **impact** (India 9th)
  [1.10] **Allianz Green Transition Tracker 2026** (05 Nov 2026) | By **Allianz Research**; **65 economies** (81% of emissions); **India 45th** (up 5 from 50th); top **Luxembourg, Denmark, Estonia**; USA 41st, Canada 42nd, China 43rd
  [1.11] India's GHG emissions (Allianz) | **Fell 0.2% in 2025** to **4,508 MtCO2e** despite about **7.6% growth** – the **first fall outside an economic contraction in 25 years**
  [1.12] Global electricity | **Renewables (33.5%) overtook coal (33.2%)**

### part 3: Peace, Security and Terrorism Reports
[0] h: Global Peace Index (GPI) 2026
[1] table: Fact | Key detail
  [1.0] Publisher | **Institute for Economics and Peace (IEP), Sydney**; annual **since 2007**
  [1.1] India | **127th of 163** (score 2.409) – **down from 115th**; reasons: internal conflicts, Pakistan/Myanmar border tensions, Manipur violence
  [1.2] Most peaceful | **Iceland (1)** – **19th year**; **New Zealand (2)**, **Switzerland (3)**
  [1.3] Least peaceful | **Russia**
  [1.4] South Asia | **Bhutan 16th** – most peaceful in the region

  [2.4] Pakistan | 152
[3] h: Global Terrorism Index (GTI) 2026
[4] table: Fact | Key detail
  [4.0] Edition | **13th edition**; publisher **Institute for Economics & Peace (IEP), Sydney**; **163 countries**
  [4.1] Ranks | **Pakistan most affected**; **India 13th**
  [4.2] 2025 trend | Deaths **down 28%** (about **5,582**); incidents **down 22%** (about **2,944**); **81 countries** improved
  [4.3] Where | **99%** of deaths in conflict-affected / unstable countries; **60%+** of attacks within **100 km of borders**
  [4.4] **West** | **93%** of fatal attacks (last 5 years) by **lone actors**
  [4.5] **Sahel** | Over half of terrorism deaths; **6 of the top 10** most affected countries in **Sub-Saharan Africa**
  [4.6] Major groups | Islamic State, JNIM, TTP, Lashkar-e-Taiba, Al-Shabaab
[5] h: India's counter-terror architecture
[6] table: Fact | Key detail
  [6.0] Terrorist act defined | **Section 15, UAPA 1967**; also **BNS 2023**
  [6.1] **PRAHAAR Framework** | **Intelligence-led, pre-emptive deterrence**; **decouples terrorism from religion**
  [6.2] 2019 laws | **UAPA Amendment** (individuals as terrorists); **NIA Amendment** (offences abroad); **Arms Amendment**
  [6.3] Institutions | **53** Special NIA Courts; **MAC** connects **28 agencies**; Cyber MAC; NATGRID; CCTNS 2.0
  [6.4] Police modernisation | **₹6,300 crore+** (2021–2026)
  [6.5] Operations | **Surgical Strikes 2016** → **Balakot 2019** → **Operation Sindoor 2025**
  [6.6] International | **FATF member since 2010**; **No Money for Terror** conference 2022; **CCIT** proposed at the UN in **1996**; JWGs with 27 countries and 5 forums; **BHARATPOL 2025**; Delhi Declaration 2022
  [6.7] J&K terror incidents | **228 (2018) → 12 (2025)**
[7] h: SIPRI Yearbook 2026
[8] table: Fact | Key detail
  [8.0] Publisher | **Stockholm International Peace Research Institute** (Stockholm, Sweden); est. **1966**
  [8.1] **Nine nuclear-armed states** | USA, Russia, UK, France, China, India, Pakistan, North Korea, Israel
  [8.2] Warheads (start 2026) | **12,187** total; **9,745** in military stockpiles; **4,000+** deployed; **US + Russia ~86%**
  [8.3] Other warheads | **China 620**, **Russia 5,420**, **USA 5,044**
  [8.4] **Operation Sindoor (May 2025)** | First time India and Pakistan **integrated cyber operations** into an active conflict
[9] table: Indicator | India
  [9.0] Nuclear warheads | ~190 (early 2026) – 12 deployed, 178 in storage; added 10 in a year; ~20 more than Pakistan
  [9.1] Military spending 2025 | 5th largest – USD 92.1 bn (+8.9%), after US, China, Russia, Germany
  [9.2] Arms imports 2021–25 | 2nd largest – 8.2% of global
[10] h: India's nuclear background
[11] table: Fact | Key detail
  [11.0] **Nuclear doctrine** | Drafted **1999**, adopted **2003** – Credible Minimum Deterrence, **No First Use**, massive retaliation, **civilian control**, no use against non-nuclear states
  [11.1] **Nuclear Command Authority** | **Political Council – chaired by PM**; **Executive Council – chaired by NSA**
  [11.2] **Pokhran-I** | **18 May 1974** – Smiling Buddha (Peaceful Nuclear Explosion)
  [11.3] **Pokhran-II** | **May 1998** – **Operation Shakti** (**5 tests**); **National Technology Day – 11 May**
  [11.4] **2008** | India–US civil nuclear deal + **NSG waiver**
  [11.5] Treaties | Has not signed the **NPT, CTBT**; **opposes TPNW (2017)**
  [11.6] Regimes | **Member of MTCR, Wassenaar Arrangement, Australia Group**; seeks NSG membership
  [11.7] Safeguards | Reactors using imported uranium are under **IAEA safeguards**
[12] x: India is a member of **MTCR, Wassenaar and the Australia Group**, not of the **NSG**; it has **not signed the NPT or CTBT**.

[8] h: World Bank – Water–Food Nexus Framework
[9] table: Fact | Key detail
  [9.0] Released | **Ahead of World Water Day 2026**; data from geospatial + household surveys across **58 countries**
  [9.1] Population | ~**10 billion by 2050**; current systems can sustainably feed only ~**3.4 billion**
  [9.2] Climate change by 2050 | Food production **down 6–14%**; up to **1.36 billion** in severe food insecurity
  [9.3] **Two dimensions** | Water stress level + position in food trade (net importer/exporter)
  [9.4] **Four categories** | Water-secure importer, water-secure exporter, **water-stressed exporter**, water-stressed importer
  [9.5] India | **Water-stressed food exporter** – groundwater over-extraction in **Punjab, Haryana, Delhi, western UP**
  [9.6] Productivity | **10%** rise in farm productivity cuts poverty by **2.5–3%**
  [9.7] Irrigation jobs | Expansion could create **245 million jobs** (218 million in Sub-Saharan Africa), ~4 jobs per hectare
  [9.8] Investment | Needed **$600 billion – $1.8 trillion**; of **$663 billion** agri spending (2023) only **$27 billion** on irrigation
  [9.9] **Drip irrigation** | Reduces **weeds and erosion** (it does not reduce salinity)

  [1.8] **Telangana Police** | Registered one of India's first cases of **human trafficking for forced cyber fraud** (flagged by **INTERPOL**)

[2] x: NCRB 2024: total crime **fell**, but **UAPA cases rose** by 12.5%.
